## 2026-10-03 - CreateSema state and real nested returns

- Implemented bounded CreateSema for zero attributes using pinned PS2SDK parameter offsets and Play! creation behavior. Reads max_count/init_count/option, ignores output-only count/wait_threads, and stores explicit count/maximum/option state. IDs begin at zero; capacity is 256 as in the pinned Play! model. No wait queues, wakeups, deletion, signal or scheduler behavior is claimed.

- MSVC build/pipeline, three CTests and all 14 Python tooling tests passed. Synthetic semaphore tests cover state/IDs, ignored output fields, preserved upper bits, invalid count, unsupported attributes, parameter memory bounds/alignment and capacity exhaustion. Neutral names retained. Added tested SRA32 to compile the reached next initialization routine. Generated FUN_0032F020, FUN_00331C38, FUN_00331D70, FUN_00331888 and FUN_00331FE8 now complete their first invocation and return to main. New handler-registration, status-check and packet-allocation helpers also return. Counts: **66 distinct entered / 62 completed** across probes; entry, FUN_00100388, FUN_0031F8B0 and FUN_00332790 remain partial. A completed function can be invoked again and stop on new inputs. Generation unchanged: 7,262 functions / 7,271 raw files.

Pinned PS2SDK sifcmd-common.h establishes CHANGE_SADDR=0x80000000, INIT_CMD=0x80000002 and RPC_BIND=0x80000009. The reached first packet is CHANGE_SADDR for an already initialized IOP command service, not INIT_CMD. Public EE initialization code selects this branch when its SUBADDR software query returns nonzero; IOP CHANGE_SADDR writes the supplied EE receive-buffer address. Public receive logic consumes the low-byte packet size. Pinned sifrpc-common.h supplies the pending bind packet field layout. These architectural/ABI observations support the authored narrow model; no vendor or retail implementation is copied into the repository, and no retail function is renamed.

Extended the isolated local PCSX2 capture tool with sif-dma-before at 0x00331D44 (marker 0xB70C) and sif-dma-after at 0x00331D4C (0xB70D), publishing v0 and SP. It reads IOPmem's exported pointer and first 2 MiB, with Main at offset zero supported by pinned PCSX2 MemoryTypes.h. Two boot captures validate the same SP=0x01FFEC70, four-word descriptor [0x003A9580,0x00019600,20,0x44] and five-word packet [20,0,0x80000000,0,0x003A94C0]. Post-call v0=0x701. IOP packet at 0x19600 becomes [0,0,0x80000000,0,0x003A94C0]; matching table at 0x196C0 starts [0x19600,0x19680], and its field at +8 changes from 0x93640 to 0x3A94C0. This table/field interpretation is supported by public layout and paired local observations. Bytes past the 20-byte packet are outside the native model's scope.

At this later boundary the full kernel prefix changed between reads, so the tool checks the reached EE packet/descriptor and IOP receive packet/table windows twice instead. Other EE kernel/IOP state remains live; independent boots are diagnostic checkpoints, not cycle-equivalent snapshots or a gameplay proof. After UTC 2026-10-03T22:39:04Z: EE 1f2335ed020023a5ecdc576d1a89fe44ec7654349b8d0c4de2a5bf6f0b156824; IOP 70aa9efbb65509ca56187d77d91cc4d5da1ee6143f1087f45e18032c51ca0691. Original ELF, BIOS, process access and installation remain unchanged/read-only; local copies/captures remain ignored.

Pipeline --sif-dma-before-capture/--sif-dma-after-capture requires both and the earlier first SIF query capture. Validator checks original ELF, EE/IOP lengths and hashes, source trap/marker, published SP/result, descriptor/packet, matching PCSX2/BIOS, table identity, previous/new EE address and consumed receive packet. Ignored bundle contains before IOP state plus captured result. Runtime validates exact descriptor, command, flags and prerequisites before mutation; copies the 20-byte packet into its isolated IOP state, consumes psize, updates the receive address field, then returns captured 0x701. Other IOP memory and all EE RAM are preserved. Unit tests verify complete memory preservation outside those effects, invalid/repeated supplies, wrong count/unknown command rejection and upper GPR preservation. This is a synchronous logical receive model for one command; no general DMA register engine, 16-byte hardware rounding/padding, queue-ID allocation, IRQ execution, timing or IOP instruction execution is claimed.

Generated FUN_00331B70 installs four handler records: command IDs 8/9/10/12 -> 0x003322E8/0x003326C0/0x003328E0/0x003323F8, each arg=0x003AAFC0 and GP=0x0038BDF0, at table 0x003A95C0 (stride 12). These addresses are registered data; the four handlers are not compiled/executed. Generated MULT leaves HI=0, LO=144, now explicitly checked. Next SifGetReg selector 0x80000002 stops at 0x0032F078. Added separate sif-rpc-state capture at caller 0x00332110/marker 0xB70E: v0=1, selector=0x80000002. EE hash 847ff17753b2b8383a545fb6f7fd505aff0814f84387a06574c7d001bc5482e6, UTC 2026-10-03T22:34:41Z. Explicit pipeline input requires the paired receive captures and matching reference PCSX2/BIOS. Runtime supplies this result only once, after the first receive, preserving upper GPR bits. It does not implement general SIF register values or initialize an RPC service from scratch.

With the explicit RPC state, generated FUN_00331FE8 returns to FUN_00100388, whose next call enters FUN_0031F8B0. Following calls complete a status check and packet allocation. The actual read blocker 0x203A97D0 at 0x003321E0 is the low-main-RAM uncached alias, defined by pinned PS2SDK UNCACHED_SEG bit 0x20000000. Checked coherent byte/word alias access in [0x20000000,0x22000000) is enabled only after the explicit RPC state query. The reached LBU at 0x00331CC8 also uses this alias, and prepared CACHE 0x18 line checks accept its bounded physical counterpart. Tests verify visibility from both aliases, byte/word semantics, alignment/bounds/pointer rejection. Wider alias accesses, MMIO, TLB and guest cache state remain unsupported.

Native execution creates semaphore ID 3 (count 0/max 1/option 0x003838D8) and prepares a 64-byte RPC_BIND packet at physical 0x003A97C0 / alias 0x203A97C0. Checked packet words include command 0x80000009, record ID 5, RPC ID 2, client address 0x003A87A8 and server ID 0x80000592. At the second SifSetDma: a0/SP=0x01FFEB90 (initial minus 464), a1=1, descriptor [0x203A97C0,0x00019600,64,0x44], resume/PC 0x0032F028, selector 0x77, handler 0. It fails before a second IOP mutation or success result. This is the new RPC service lookup/response subsystem; server identity/purpose and required response/delivery are still unknown. No fake server pointer, completion, response packet, callback or semaphore signal is added.

Full checked probe: 129 guest calls, 122 JR returns, 1,093 word stores (668 kernel), 107 doublewords, 43,632 quadwords, 529 bytes. New counts: one logical SIF receive, one RPC state replay, 11 uncached word accesses, two uncached byte accesses, two checked cache lines. Earlier counts remain: 17 custom callbacks, 18 syscall registrations, 12 copied-kernel validations, six cache requests/two I-cache invalidations, one wait/signal, one D_STAT/CHCR read, one DMAC handler/enable request. Four semaphores, one dormant thread; Status=0x10000. Full original baseline/payload/zero/buffer/thread/OSD/INTC/timer assertions remain checked; active new call frames, registration table, IOP effects and pending RPC descriptor are validated.

Verification: intermediate MSVC builds/reruns, full checkpoint pipeline, seven CTests, 40 recomp tooling tests and fifteen capture-tool tests passed. Optional boundaries retained: omit RPC state -> RPCINIT query; omit paired DMA receive inputs -> first SifSetDma; no SIF query -> earlier first SifGetReg. ## 2026-10-05 - Absent-card SIO2 DMA transport and timed-wait boundary

## 2026-10-06 - First native disc metadata lookup and next command frontier

Followed the bounded DISC_SEARCH_PLAN through multiple blockers: validated boot-service bind/reply, generated bind semaphore cleanup, first synchronous call semaphore, translated CDVDFSV handler, owned search serialization, ISO metadata provider, original secondary submission, primary response, generated callback/cleanup and uncached metadata copy. The local IOPRP equals the disc image; CDVDFSV SHA256 is efff610e5865357260d55409025fd0c7d518c62be4c268b2a9923b978a1ab8e6. All relocated code ranges match independent retail IOP RAM at base 0x3DD30. Captured service descriptor 0x433D0 identifies service 0x80000597, handler 0x3E484 and buffer 0x441C8; module metadata confirms GP 0x4B0B0. Authored native placement 0x1A0000 / GP 0x1AD380 uses checked disjoint RAM. Only the verified handler is entered; CDVDFSV boot entry, worker registration and residency are not claimed. Scoped providers are restored after execution or error.

Independent before/after EE captures establish the 300-byte command-zero request, layer zero, client 0x3A8780, input/output 0x3A8600 and primary result pointer 0x3A8740. The first path is `\DATA\ZS2US.DIR;1`. Original handler selects CDVDMAN ordinal 84 and sends a 36-byte SIF descriptor [0x441C8,0x3A8600,36,0]. A terminal observer executes the real first submission without redirecting its source, retains submission bytes and verifies the EE destination. It terminates immediately after submission return; it does not observe a DMA poll or handler return. The independent after-return capture verifies success 1 and metadata LBA 0xE2A / size 64 / terminated filename / binary date (2006-07-13 09:49:51). Native defined fields and original result slot match; undefined filename padding is not equated.

The read-only authored ISO9660 backend validates paired endian fields, record/sector boundaries, physical extents, supported record shapes, directory size, exact path/version, ambiguity and date ranges. It provides metadata only. Synthetic fixtures check lookup, missing files, invalid paths, corrupt extent/volume pairs and invalid dates. The service remains deliberately scoped to this first request and rejects changed epoch, size, path, layer or revision metadata before execution. No file payload, sector-completion API, repeated-search support or host remapping of arbitrary filenames is supplied.

The pinned public SC(-10) contract acquires/clears a search bit for the original 0xFF parameter, releases it for zero and returns the parameter. This adapter owns one serialized request. Initial development incorrectly expected a DMA poll before release; original handler instructions show it accepts a nonzero submission token and does not call sceSifDmaStat on this path. The assumption was removed. Native secondary completion is logical; physical IOP DMA timing is not equated. Earlier observer variants caught shared-stub traffic or timed out; those diagnostics grant no completion and stay ignored. The final observer verifies only the actual first submission and destination.

Generated EE LDL/LDR copies first use uncached alias 0x203A8600. A bounded read64 adapter allows only the owned first 32 bytes after query completion; tests reject pre-completion, unaligned and out-of-buffer reads. Existing word aliases handle the final flags word and primary result. The generated search-lock release adds a signal beyond the synchronous RPC lifecycle and is accounted for at the completed-query checkpoint.

Final native checkpoint: **1,463 EE calls / 1,452 returns**, **41,861 IOP instructions / 164 physical targets**, twelve ordinary waiters plus the parked reverse EE bind. One disc bind, one original search handler, one 36-byte submission, zero DMA-stat calls, result 1. Fourteen module-load paths / thirteen resident modules remain unchanged. The older distinct-function checkpoint remains 111 entered / 107 completed; no new distinct total was measured.

The next checked boundary is CreateSema selector 0x40 / PC 0x32EC68, return 0x332814, descriptor 0x1FFEB00, client 0x369A90, service 0x80000595, RPC ID 44. Public libcdvd identifies the command service; this is not a completed bind or read. ## 2026-10-06 — main priority admission, helper first waits and VBlank registration

Normal startup advanced from 302/295 to **429 entered / 420 completed**, kernel 2/2, **4,729 calls / 4,723 returns**. IOP instructions remain 81,640 (214 physical targets); CRI calls 37; heaps six. New checked boundary: main's first idle call in its asynchronous request poll loop, `0x1007F0 → 0x2EF218`.

**Scheduler.** Main's ChangeThreadPriority(1, 24) is admitted before dispatch: priority becomes 24 and the original previous priority 2 is returned (capture `rpc-main-priority-after`: v0 = 2). Every ready thread above 24 runs first, in priority order, on persistent owned stacks. After admission, dispatched workers and main share one bounded thread layer: ChangeThreadPriority (TH_SELF, previous-priority return), ReferThreadStatus (RUN/READY/WAIT/SUSPEND/WAITSUSPEND/DORMANT; status word only), SuspendThread, ResumeThread and worker SleepThread. Any transition that would let a strictly higher-priority ready thread preempt fails closed and rolls back; equal priorities keep FIFO order. The `--startup-workers` diagnostic gate and the provisional helper-5 handlers were removed.

**Helpers.** Helper 5 (priority 16) and helper 6 (18) each use the shared critical section 0x2EDC10 / 0x2EDC98 ten times. Each use raises the caller to priority 1, checks and resumes suspended helper 4, runs the body, then suspends helper 4 and restores the priority. Overall there are 28 balanced resume/suspend pairs and 56 priority changes (main included). The helpers park at their first SleepThread with SP 0x34E430 / RA 0x2EE00C and SP 0x34F430 / RA 0x2EE100, GP 0x38BDF0, matching original first-wait captures. The rewritten `verify_startup_workers.py` compares nine original observer points (main after; helper critical before/after, restore before and first wait). Helper 6's conditional BGCOLOR store is skipped because flag 0x34BC84 is zero, as captured.

**CRI stream copies.** Helper 5 fills and submits raw SifSetDma copies of the stream buffers registered by original CRI create commands 0 and 1 (EE 0x365BC0 → IOP 0x14FD40; EE 0x35FD00 → IOP 0x191740; 0x880 bytes, attribute 0). The receiver checks the EE stream object (slot 0x1B58C0 / 0x1B5904, buffer, IOP target, size, idle state) and native IOP ownership. It copies the bytes and returns an authored logical token. Each copy follows one full indexed cache writeback. The 0x40-byte trailer carries an EE sequence counter; IOP consumption and acknowledgement are not modeled.

**Main continuation.** Main registers a callback slot under the same critical section. It then installs VBLANK_START handler 0x1006D8 (`next=-1`, tail order, caller GP recorded) and enables INTC cause 2 (checkpoint mask 0x1802; bit 2 set). No interrupt is raised or delivered. Main starts an asynchronous request and polls it; the idle path services a 32-slot EE handle table. An unbounded run exhausted the execution budget with no IOP request, so the probe now stops at the first idle call. EE_INTERRUPT_LOAD_PLAN.md defines the next subsystem.

**Map tooling.** Focused Ghidra discovery now separates direct `J` tail-call targets absorbed into an observed function's body when the target is a call target or opens a stack frame within two instructions (0x2F2718 → 0x2F22F0; wrapper 0x2EAFE0 → 0x2EB030). `prepare_map.py` now proves a second switch layout (moved index; BEQL with an annulled delay slot). It also accepts padding NOPs after a proven switch jump or `JR RA`, while still rejecting branches into gaps. These changes recovered about 90 previously quarantined candidates. Map: 8,687 entries / 8,690 raw files; 371 selected extras. Every receipt refresh verified unchanged hashes for previously selected raw sources.

**Verification.** Serialized MSVC entry/ALL builds; **17/17 CTests** (new bootstrap scheduler/VBlank-registration cases); **157 capture**, **57 recompilation** and **13 IOP** Python tests; 17 normal-startup comparisons including the new helper comparison. The separate sound-worker diagnostic is unchanged at 302/295 and verified. `verify_ee_main_status.py`, `verify_ee_cache.py` and `verify_cri_autodma.py` are now phase-aware: admitted priority, transferred stream buffer equal to its IOP copy, and one extra indexed scan per transfer. `verify_disc_read.py` and `verify_spu2_initialization.py` already fail on the previous pass's late reports and are left for a tooling pass. Two selection loops briefly overlapped during the pass. Receipt/provenance checks were rerun afterwards, and every final build/run was serialized.

No interrupt delivery, thread wake, request completion, archive payload, audio playback or presented frame is claimed. Authored changes only; captures, generated code and game inputs remain ignored.


## 2026-10-07 — VBlank delivery, helper wakes and the first archive load

Normal startup advanced from 429/420 to **510 entered / 499 completed**, kernel 2/2, **74,373 calls / 74,359 returns**, **138,268 IOP instructions / 216 targets**. CRI calls 37, heaps six. New boundary: main's CRI command `0x408` (38th call) after the first archive load.

**Evidence.** Three new original observer points use the existing PCSX2 capture tool. `rpc-vblank-first` shows the first VBLANK_START handler entry after EnableIntc(2) and before main's next call and request (callback slot and request still zero). The handler runs with a0 = 2, a1 = registered argument 0x8001A5B8, GP 0x38BDF0, kernel SP 0x81FC0 and EIE clear. `rpc-load-poll-idle` (main's first idle call) shows exactly one VBlank and both helpers on their second iteration. `rpc-load-poll-done` shows the request completing after 120 VBlanks, with the 0x6880-byte table at 0x635500.

**Interrupts and wakes.** VBLANK_START latches while masked, so enabling cause 2 delivers it at the enable wrapper's EI. Handlers run in chain order with the kernel ABI on the captured stack; a negative result ends the chain. Interrupt context handles iGetThreadId (−47), iReferThreadStatus (−49) and iWakeupThread (−52); other syscalls fail closed. After the handler, ready threads above main run. Parked helper stacks resume only after an owned wake, and SleepThread returns the caller ID. Play! (PS2 HLE reference) confirms the wake/sleep and handler-chain conventions. At main's first idle call, native VBlank/helper/request state equals the original. The only difference is the stream trailer counters: the original IOP consumer has acknowledged with 2; natively they stay at the EE value 1 (IOP consumption unmodeled).

**Logical time and the load.** At each idle call in main's poll, one VBlank is delivered (cap 1,000; then a checked boundary). Helper 6 drives the CRI file system. It makes repeated NCMD DiskReady calls through the original CDVDFSV handler (idle drive register readback reused), and generalised NCMD reads (per-request LBN/count/destination/mode; sceCdRV arg5 = cache sector count). It also makes SCMD GetError calls via the newly translated CDVDFSV S-command handler (retail 0x41BC8, checked against the original registration; driver error 0). The first read returns the `AFS\0` header from LBN 0x1FC26, matching the ISO. After 105 logical VBlanks and 53 reads, main leaves the poll. Its loaded 0x6880-byte archive table is **byte-identical** to the original capture. Phase-scoped admission covers SIF RPC library temporary semaphores (attr residue) and their cleanup. Report-time pre-admission accounting invariants are now scoped to their phase. The IOP runaway guard rose from 100 thousand to 50 million lifetime steps.

**FPU.** The first reached COP1 code needed CVT/MUL/SUB/SQRT and comparisons. `ps2_fpu.cpp` implements the EE FPU's non-IEEE semantics: round toward zero (PCSX2's default chop), no Inf/NaN (overflow clamps to ±max), denormals flush to zero, and saturating CVT.W.S. It covers every FPU macro the generated program uses (ADD, SUB, MUL, SQRT, ABS, NEG, MOV, CVT.S.W, CVT.W.S, C.EQ/OLT/OLE). Flags are not modeled; `div.s` is still emitted inline with IEEE behaviour.

**Map tooling.** The switch prover now accepts any non-control index producer (safety rests on the SLTIU bound and guard). Map: 8,699 entries / 8,702 files / 440 selected; every receipt refresh checked unchanged selected hashes.

**Verification.** Serialized MSVC entry/ALL builds; **17/17 CTests** (new interrupt-delivery, wake and FPU cases); **157 capture**, **57 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests. 19 normal-startup comparisons pass, including the new VBlank/load comparison. Six earlier verifiers became phase-aware: an open critical section at the boundary, later readiness/read counts, a reused IOP result slot, an in-flight CRI submission and grown cache-scan counts. The sound-worker diagnostic is unchanged at 302/295 and verified. No IOP stream acknowledgement, sound command processing, archive decompression, audio playback or presented frame is claimed.

## 2026-10-07 — late CRI pairs, repeated clear, embedded reload and display setup

Normal startup advanced from 511/502 to **531 entered / 524 completed**, kernel 2/2, **75,544 calls / 75,538 returns**, **156,780 IOP instructions / 248 targets**, **42 CRI calls**, 7 heap calls / 2 frees, 15 module loads.

**Late CRI pairs.** Mapping `0x2F6878` exposed a leaf tail-call target (`0x2F1360`, `JR RA` + delay slot) absorbed by Ghidra. Discovery now also separates a `J` target placed directly after a completed `JR RA` pair. The remaining four second-channel pairs then completed through the original IOP handler; all five pair objects match the original (`verify_cri_second_channel.py`, full checkpoint).

**Repeated startup clear.** Main calls its startup clear routine (`0x100258`) again after the archive load. The GS reset sees the registered VSync callback, disables cause 2 and removes the handler (RemoveIntcHandler). SyncV then polls INTC_STAT directly: a poll that finds VBLANK_START clear advances one logical VBlank (cause 2 stays disabled; FIELD toggles). Both GIF clear requests replay the original packet evidence from a fresh staging state into the shared GS sink. `0x271178` samples the field; the stored flag matches the original (field 1).

**Embedded reload.** `0x320580` reloads the embedded 864-byte IRX. The original placed it at IOP `0x196100` (image `0x196530`, module ID 39, result 1, then freed), recovered from the new `rpc-display-init-after` IOP RAM. `generate_embedded.py` now validates and translates a second placement from that capture; the native heap, copy, DMA poll, LOADFILE buffer load and free accept this second cycle only. The heap client RPC ID is matched live.

**Display setup.** `0x271078` resets the GS again (no handler to remove), sets CRT (interlaced NTSC, frame mode), builds the 512×448 double-buffer environment and re-registers the VSync callback with a fresh handler ID. The 0x240-byte environment is byte-identical to the original; GS library state matches except the model-local handler ID (native 4, original 5).

**Boundary.** Main then reads EE Timer 2 COUNT for its system time (`0x339430`). The original result (`rpc-timer-seed-after`) is `0x1C825400`. Timer 2 counting and overflow interrupts are a new subsystem ([plan](../plans/ee/EE_TIMER_PLAN.md)); the read is a checked boundary.

## 2026-10-07 — logical Timer 2 and the first synchronous file load

Normal startup advanced from 531/524 to **564 entered / 556 completed**, **80,978 calls / 80,912 returns**, **159,101 IOP instructions**, 54 original disc reads.

**Timer 2.** Main's system-time read (`0x339410`) combines Timer 2 COUNT with a software overflow count kept by the timer library's cause-11 handler (`0x339118`). A logical Timer 2 (`runtime/probe_abi/logical_timer2.h`) now starts from the startup mode (`0x382`, BUSCLK/256) at COUNT 0 / COMP 0; the BIOS-era residue is unobservable because the initial mode already had CUE set. It advances one NTSC field (9,609.6 ticks, exact fixed point) at every logical VBlank, latches EQUF/OVFF, and delivers cause 11 before the VBlank handlers at the same point. The original handler reads COUNT/MODE, acknowledges OVFF/EQUF through its kernel-mode register helper and sets COMP 0. Sixteen wraps were delivered and acknowledged. Library state matches the original (`rpc-timer-seed-after`) except the overflow count (original 28, logical time) and the model-local handler ID; the seed is reported, not equated (`verify_timer2.py`).

**Synchronous file load.** Main's load helper (`0x100CB8`) polls the same file service from a second idle site (`0x100D20`); it now advances logical time like the first poll. Its read completed through the original CDVDFSV handlers (54th read).

**Boundary.** Helper 6 then requests 52 sectors. The original FSV handler streams requests above 16 sectors through a 16-sector ring in chunks; the owned read model covers one transfer. The request now stops before the IOP is entered, and the native read limit matches the cache count (16).

Verified with serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests** (new Timer 2 unit test), **165 capture**, **57 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **21 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). No presented frame, decompression or playback is claimed.


## 2026-10-07 — streamed CDVDFSV reads

Normal startup advanced from 564/556 to **565 entered / 557 completed**, **83,789 calls / 83,734 returns**, **165,606 IOP instructions**, **55 original disc reads**.

Helper 6 requested 52 sectors. The original FSV handler issues one `sceCdRV` with a 16-sector ring cache and callback `0x1A0B00`, then per chunk synchronizes, checks sector headers, SIF-DMAs the chunk to its EE offset and reports progress; between chunks it clears event bit 0x20 and reprograms CDVD DMA channel 3 (MADR = ring, BCR = (43 × sectors) << 16 | 12, CHCR start). The owned read model now follows this protocol: the RV fills the first chunk; each DMA restart streams the next chunk from the local ISO into the ring start; each chunk's synchronization raises the original callback; the read completes with the last chunk. The block layout (12-word blocks, 43 per sector) comes from the retail CDVDMAN workspace in the existing captures. The 52-sector destination equals the ISO. The bounded ISO reader now accepts up to 512 sectors. Generated code needed further checked fast-path memory aliases (`FAST_WRITE16` and siblings).

Verified with serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, **165 capture**, **57 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **21 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). No presented frame, decompression or playback is claimed.


## 2026-10-07 — post-load setup and the first sound-block upload

Normal startup advanced from 565/557 to **584 entered / 576 completed**, **92,090 calls / 92,028 returns**, **172,691 IOP instructions**, **57 original disc reads**, 117 logical VBlanks, 17 Timer 2 overflows.

Main's post-load setup read two more files through the streamed CDVDFSV path and then uploaded a 0x16640-byte block from EE `0x8CC180` into the IOP sound buffer at `0xCE500` (owned by the second heap allocation): one SIF DMA descriptor from `0x28BA18`, then a status poll. A bounded receiver accepts this upload only in main's admitted context after display setup, with attribute 0 and a destination inside native IOP memory; the IOP bytes equal the EE source. Generated code needed further checked fast-path memory macros.

Main then sends sound-driver command `0x80D0` to service `0x80000701`. Executing it through the original handler reaches LIBSD's voice transfer (SPU2 DMA4 with IRQ 36 completion), which the owned SPU2 device does not model: the request is validated on the EE side and stops before the IOP is entered. A plan for SPU2 voice transfers is in [IOP_SPU2_TRANSFER_PLAN.md](../plans/sound/IOP_SPU2_TRANSFER_PLAN.md). `verify_timer2.py` now allows later overflows after the seed sample.

Verified with serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, **165 capture**, **57 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **21 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). No presented frame, decompression or playback is claimed.


## 2026-10-07 — SPU2 voice transfers and the first sound-bank blocks

Normal startup advanced from 584/576 to **585 entered / 576 completed**, **92,166 calls / 92,104 returns**, **173,374 IOP instructions**.

**SPU2 voice transfers.** Sound-driver command `0x80D0` reaches LIBSD's transfer routine. While a later sound command runs, an owned SPU2 voice-DMA model handles the transfer address, attribute DMA bits, the SSBUS DMA timing nibble and DMA4/DMA7 MADR/BCR/CHCR (core 1 uses DMA7 at `0x1F801500`). A CHCR start copies the IOP bytes into SPU2 sound RAM at the transfer address and runs LIBSD's registered IRQ 36/40 handler at once, on a stack below the live stack pointer so the issuing frames stay intact. `QueryIntrContext` (`intrman` 23) is provided. Two sound-driver commands complete through the original handlers. A unit test covers the register sequence, rejection of read transfers and unowned sources, and handler ordering.

**Sound-bank blocks.** The loader (`0x297A60`) then allocates two IOP blocks (0x2C0 and 0x180 bytes). The new `rpc-sound-bank-after` capture places them first-fit in the freed reload block at `0x196100` and `0x196400`; the native allocator uses these placements and both uploads match the original (`verify_sound_bank.py`, which also compares the 0x16640-byte sound block, the SOUNDS request record and the loader slot table).

**Boundary.** The final SOUNDS command `0x20` starts a higher-priority SOUNDS worker, wakes it and converts a period (scoped SleepThread/WakeupThread/iWakeupThread and USec2SysClock providers, installed only during later sound commands), then calls `timrman` AllocHardTimer. IOP hardware timers are not modelled, so the command now stops before the IOP is entered ([plan](../plans/iop/IOP_HARDWARE_TIMER_PLAN.md)). Four verifiers were made phase-aware for the later sound and heap calls.

Verified with serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests** (new SPU2 transfer test), **168 capture**, **57 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **22 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). No audio output, presented frame or decompression is claimed.


## 2026-10-07 — IOP hardware timer, SOUNDS worker and main's frame loop

Normal startup advanced from 585/576 to **591 entered / 582 completed**, **92,201 calls / 92,138 returns**, **198,905 IOP instructions**. Main now reaches its frame loop.

**IOP hardware timer.** SOUNDS command `0x20` starts the SOUNDS worker, which sleeps, then sets up a 4,167 µs SYSCLK hardware timer (AllocHardTimer, SetTimerHandler, SetupHardTimer, StartHardTimer) whose handler wakes it. TIMRMAN providers, installed only during later sound commands, record the timer with the captured placement (root counter 5, id `0x5BF80149`, from `rpc-sound-bank-after` IOP RAM). The timer advances one NTSC field per EE logical VBlank and delivers each elapsed interval to the original handler in interrupt context, then runs woken workers until they park (unit-tested; no tick has run in normal startup yet). Scoped SleepThread/WakeupThread/iWakeupThread and USec2SysClock (36.864 MHz) providers carry the worker start. The command's reply matches the original apart from a model-local thread ID and the relocated SOUNDS address (`verify_sound_bank.py`).

**Frame loop and file I/O.** Main's loader returns and its frame loop starts. The first frame step (`0x1002D8`) opens `cdrom0:\BIN\DBZP.BIN` with the C library's file API to read the whole file. The lazily created stdio lock ("SceStdioFioSema") is admitted; stdio initialization then binds the IOP FILEIO server (`0x80000001`), which is not translated: the bind is a checked boundary ([plan](../plans/ee/EE_FILEIO_PLAN.md)). Report semaphore accounting excludes the stdio lock and no longer applies startup counts after display setup. Further verifiers were made phase-aware for later SOUNDS commands.

Verified with serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests** (new IOP timer test), **168 capture**, **57 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **22 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). No audio output, presented frame or decompression is claimed.


## 2026-10-07 — FILEIO bind and initialization

Normal startup advanced from 591/582 to **595 entered / 587 completed**, **92,247 calls / 92,184 returns**.

The C library's stdio initialization binds boot FILEIO's file server (`0x80000001`). Its retail placement is validated in captured IOP RAM: descriptor `0x47788` (handler `0x46298` = retail FILEIO base `0x44430` + `0x1E68`), buffer `0x477D0`; the bind replies with these, as the heap bind does. The first request (`0xFF`, registering EE result buffers `0x3ABCC0`/`0x3AC100`) runs through the original handler on an owned stack with the request copied into its native receive buffer (`NativeIopService::callFileio`, with scoped IOP→EE SIF DMA providers). The per-open "SceStdioOpenSema" is admitted alongside the stdio lock.

The open of `cdrom0:\BIN\DBZP.BIN;1` is asynchronous: FILEIO allocates a 0xC40-byte request record and hands it to a worker that later reports through IOP→EE SIF commands. The open is validated on the EE side and stops before the IOP is entered. A new capture, `rpc-dbzp-loaded`, shows the original result: `DBZP.BIN` (0x7D48F bytes) at EE `0x3B3100`, byte-identical to the ISO, with FILEIO's 16 KB read buffer at IOP `0x1E7100`. Findings and next steps are in [EE_FILEIO_PLAN.md](../plans/ee/EE_FILEIO_PLAN.md).

Verified with serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, **168 capture**, **57 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **22 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). No audio output, presented frame or decompression is claimed.


## 2026-10-07 — FILEIO worker path prepared

No checkpoint change (595 / 587). FILEIO-scoped IOP message boxes, IOP->EE SIF command delivery to the C library's handler, the IOMAN `cdrom0:` device and model-local FILEIO allocations are implemented behind `fileio_workers_supported` (off). Enabled, the DBZP.BIN open runs FILEIO's dispatcher and starts its worker, which stops at its first IOP semaphore wait. Verified with serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, **168 capture**, **57 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **22 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic.


## 2026-10-07 — FILEIO workers and the DBZP.BIN load

Normal startup advanced from 595/587 to **601 entered / 595 completed** (**92,392 calls / 92,336 returns**, 203,146 IOP instructions).

**What runs now.** The C library loads `DBZP.BIN` completely through boot FILEIO: open, lseek to the end, lseek to the start, a 0x7D48F-byte read and close. Each request runs the original dispatcher. The open creates a per-file worker thread; later requests reach it through its message box. The worker serves IOMAN `cdrom0:` from the local ISO, sends read data by SIF DMA, and reports through IOP→EE SIF command `0x80000011`. That command is delivered to the C library's handler (`0x333378`), which signals the call's semaphore. The loaded image equals the original (`rpc-dbzp-loaded`, new `verify_dbzp_load.py`).

**What was added.**
- **FILEIO module-init objects.** Its module entry is not executed natively, so its two kernel objects are created as that entry does: the SIF-command lock semaphore at base+`0x3300` and the buffer event flag at base+`0x3304`. Both IDs are present at the same offsets in captured IOP RAM.
- **Mailbox fix.** The FILEIO-scoped ReceiveMbx now takes the IOP argument order `(message, mbx)`.
- **Scoped ExitThread.** It lets the worker end after the close.
- **Per-call semaphores.** The C library's per-call stdio semaphores (Open/Close/Lseek/Read) are admitted for creation and newest-first deletion.
- **Switch-table proof.** The handler's 25-way switch uses a second compiler layout (bound register, `lui` in the branch delay slot, jump through `a0`). `prepare_map.py`'s bounded switch proof now accepts it with decoded registers and the same table/target checks (unit-tested).

**Next.** Main then calls `0x3B7FA8` inside the loaded overlay. `DBZP.BIN` is raw code linked for `0x3B3100`, right after the executable's last section, and nothing outside the executable is analysed or generated yet. Plan: [EE_OVERLAY_PLAN.md](../plans/ee/EE_OVERLAY_PLAN.md).

**Verification.** Serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, **170 capture**, **58 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **23 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). No overlay execution, audio output or presented frame is claimed.


## 2026-10-07 — DBZP.BIN overlay execution

Normal startup advanced from 601/595 to **608 entered / 597 completed** (92,401 calls / 92,339 returns).

**Overlay pipeline.** `DBZP.BIN` is a raw code image linked for `0x3B3100`. It is now handled as a separate generated program (`tools/ps2recomp/overlay_programs.py`, [EE_OVERLAY_PLAN.md](../plans/ee/EE_OVERLAY_PLAN.md)):
1. **Extract.** It is extracted from the local ISO and checked against its pinned size and SHA-256, then wrapped as a minimal ELF (one executable `.text` at its link address).
2. **Analyse and map.** `analyze_game.py`, `prepare_map.py` and `generate_game.py` take `--program dbzp` and use their own Ghidra project, exports, map and generated output. Ghidra finds 482 functions; the map keeps 380, with the usual structural quarantine.
3. **Classify.** `inspect_blocker.py` classifies targets inside the overlay range against the overlay map, and discovery follows the blocker's program.
4. **Select and bind.** `expand_startup.py` records reached overlay entries in their own receipt. It emits bindings that register each function with the exact bytes it was generated from; `prepare_runtime.py` routes them like executable entries.
5. **Guard.** At runtime, `registerOverlayFunction` admits a dispatch only while RAM holds those bytes, checked on every dispatch (unit-tested, including refusal before loading and after any change).
6. **Branch kind.** Plain `J` jumps out of the overlay are tail jumps (`DirectJump`).

**Frame path.** The overlay entry and its frame code then call executable code:
- **Timer 1.** Main's H-BLANK delay helpers now use a logical Timer 1. MODE 3 is already admitted; CUE starts and stops it. COUNT advances one HBLANK per poll (262.5 lines per NTSC field), and crossing a field boundary delivers the logical VBlank first. The poll loop is unit-tested but not reached yet.
- **Display flip.** The GS library's display-environment flip records PMODE, SMODE2, DISPFB2, DISPLAY2 and BGCOLOR as privileged display state, accepted only as whole flips in order.

**Comparison with the original.** New capture `rpc-overlay-dispenv` (entry of that flip) and `verify_overlay_dispenv.py` show the native state matches: the display/double-buffer block (`0x3B0850`, 0x250 bytes), the entire overlay range and the recorded registers. The display is 512×448 interlaced.

**Next.** The draw-environment send (`0x3100D0`) is the first per-frame GIF transfer and stops as a checked memory boundary. Plan: [FRAME_GIF_PLAN.md](../plans/graphics/FRAME_GIF_PLAN.md).

**Verification.** Serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, **173 capture**, **62 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **24 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). No rendered or presented frame is claimed.


## 2026-10-07 — first overlay frame: GIF, VIF1 and display setup

Normal startup advanced from 608/597 to **620 entered / 609 completed** (92,419 calls / 92,358 returns).

**Frame GIF DMA** ([FRAME_GIF_PLAN.md](../plans/graphics/FRAME_GIF_PLAN.md)).
- **Channel 2.** In the frame phase (after the first display flip), channel 2 takes normal-mode transfers from main RAM in main's thread and completes them synchronously.
- **GS path.** The GS sink gained a general transfer path. It parses GIFtags in PACKED, REGLIST and IMAGE form (IMAGE still refused), keeps the full GS register file with a vertex queue, and draws flat untextured sprites into the owned VRAM. Anything else stops as a checked boundary.
- **No partial effects.** A whole transfer is validated in a dry run first, so a rejected transfer changes nothing (unit-tested).
- **First frame.** Its draw-environment packet clears the 512×448 framebuffer (229,376 pixels).

**Paths and display.**
- **Path sync.** The GS library's path sync sees idle paths: the captured startup idle values, since no VIF1/VU1 work is started.
- **Render kick.** The game's render kick (`0x28B318`) starts VIF1 chain DMA. It is walked synchronously through a VIF1 interpreter that accepts only NOP so far; this frame's chain is a single empty terminator.
- **Display registers.** The privileged display register file now takes the game's own display setup (both read circuits; EXTWRITE off only).
- **Doubleword MMIO.** Generated doubleword accesses through registers now take the checked MMIO path like constant-address ones (`WRITE64`/`READ64`).
- **COP2 condition.** The BC2T/BC2F condition is routed to the runtime (no VU work exists in the frame model).

**Map preparation.** Coprocessor arithmetic (COP1/COP2 with rs ≥ 16) is no longer treated as control flow in padding proofs; BC0/BC1/BC2 branches are checked like other conditional branches (unit-tested). The main map grows from 8,702 to **9,405** entries (quarantine 855 → 152) and the overlay map from 380 to 425.

**Next.** The frame's Timer 1 wait (now polled: 263 lines) crosses its first field boundary. That logical VBlank would run the IOP SOUNDS hardware timer for the first time; diagnostically, its worker reads SPU2 ENDX (core 0 voice end flags), which needs voice playback state. The VBlank is held as a checked EE boundary (`sounds_timer_ticks_supported`) before any field or IOP effect. Plan: [SPU2_VOICE_PLAN.md](../plans/sound/SPU2_VOICE_PLAN.md).

**Verification.** Verified with serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, **174 capture**, **63 recompilation**, **13 IOP** and 3 Ghidra Python tests, **24 normal-startup comparisons** and the unchanged sound-worker diagnostic (302/295). `verify_overlay_dispenv.py` still matches at this later checkpoint (display block and overlay range). No presented frame, audio or VU execution is claimed.

## 2026-10-07 — SPU2 playback verified; first overlay asset, controller ports, second frame

**Verification of the interrupted checkpoint (`149466c`).** All of these passed:
- builds and 17/17 CTests;
- Python suites: 175 capture, 63 recompilation, 13 IOP and 3 Ghidra tests;
- the sound-worker diagnostic (302/295);
- the 22 standard comparisons that do not depend on phase.

The other three (`verify_dbzp_load`, `verify_overlay_dispenv`, `verify_frame_vsync`) compare earlier phases. A new diagnostic phase stop (`BT2_PHASE_STOP`: the first entry to, or return to, an address, stopping as a branch boundary) reproduces their phases. All three match the original there, as does `verify_display_reset`. The SPU2 position/ENDX model, sound-timer interval splitting and the first frame's one-shot VSync are therefore verified as far as the plan states.

**Indexed-asset poll.** The overlay's asset loop (`0x3BC4C8`) polls a loader state machine (`0x100D58`):
- state 0: open and start the read;
- state 1: check the size;
- state 2: wait for the CRI file status to reach 3;
- state 3: close.

It makes no idle call, so the run exhausted the execution budget in state 2. Each status poll (`0x2EC990` from `0x100E38`, main thread, frame phase) is now a logical idle point that receives one VBLANK_START, the same model as main's earlier load polls.

**Disc readiness semaphore.** The woken helper's disc readiness query then used RPC client semaphore 13 instead of 10. The library creates one per blocking call, at the lowest free ID. The NCMD readiness receiver now uses the client's own ID. The read completes: 58 original disc reads.

**Controller ports.** Main's frame code sends no-wait 144-byte requests on the third and fourth DBC clients (services `0x8000131E`/`0x8000131F`, functions 0 and 1). Each runs through the original registered IOP handler; RPC_END is delivered in logical time.

**Second frame.** The second frame's Timer 1 wait and VSync complete. Its render kick then submits the first VIF1 chain with content. The VIF1 walker now refuses any non-NOP code as a checked boundary at the channel start, after a whole-chain dry run with no partial effect (unit-tested). The chain is fully PATH2 DIRECT GS traffic: a PSMT4 512×128 texture uploaded as CT32, a CSM1 CLUT and one textured, blended sprite. Plan: [GS_TEXTURE_PLAN.md](../plans/graphics/GS_TEXTURE_PLAN.md).

**Accounting.** VBlank accounting now counts Timer 1 field crossings explicitly (`timer1_vblanks`); `verify_vblank_wake` holds at 123 deliveries. Stops record the live guest return stack (`stop_returns`).

**Result.** Normal startup advanced from 620/609 to **642 entered / 633 completed** (97,963 calls / 97,899 returns, 229,661 IOP instructions, 123 logical VBlanks, 18 Timer 2 overflows).

**Verification.**
- **Builds and tests:** serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, and Python suites (**175 capture**, **63 recompilation**, **13 IOP**, 3 Ghidra).
- **Comparisons:** **26 normal-startup comparisons** plus four phase-matched ones. `verify_cri_autodma` and `verify_dbc_rpc` also hold at the frame-end phase.
- **Diagnostic:** the sound-worker diagnostic is unchanged (302/295).

No presented frame, audio output, asset-byte equivalence or VU execution is claimed.


## 2026-10-08 — first rendered screen: VIF1 DIRECT, GS textures and the controller-missing message

**Pipeline.** Implemented [GS_TEXTURE_PLAN.md](../plans/graphics/GS_TEXTURE_PLAN.md):
- **VIF1:** chains are parsed with real code lengths. NOP and FLUSH/FLUSHE/FLUSHA have no effect without VU1 work. DIRECT/DIRECTHL payloads, quadword-aligned and within one tag, reach the GS. Other codes stop at the channel start.
- **GS transactions:** all of a chain's DIRECT payloads form one transaction. The GS sink snapshots registers, CLUT, VRAM and counters, and restores them if any part is refused (replacing the dry-run approach, which could not validate CLUT loads that read just-uploaded VRAM).
- **GIF parsing:** parsing continues after EOP within a transfer, because a DIRECT payload carries several GIF packets.
- **Image transfers:** host→local IMAGE transfers into CT32. A TRXDIR write restarts a transfer.
- **Texture addressing:** PSMT4 uses the public column layout. My first formula, written from memory, scrambled the glyphs. Decoding the dumped texture offline exposed it, and the corrected bit-placement formula matches the reference layout at all 512 block positions. Unit tests now use reference entries.
- **CLUT:** CSM1 loads with CT32 entries.
- **Drawing:** textured, blended sprites (modulate/decal, nearest/bilinear, REPEAT/CLAMP, the general blend equation with COLCLAMP/PABE), plus synthetic unit tests (texture/CLUT upload, draw, transaction rollback, VIF1 DIRECT and refusals).

**Result.** From the second frame on, each frame uploads a 512×128 PSMT4 texture (as 256×32 CT32 words) and its 16-entry CLUT and draws one textured sprite. The probe dumps the owned GS memory (`.gs.bin`). The new `render_gs_frame.py` renders the displayed framebuffer to a PNG: fully written, legible, "No DUALSHOCK®2 analog controller connected. Verify that a DUALSHOCK®2 analog controller is properly inserted into controller port 1." Both controller-port requests report no device each frame, so the game repeats this screen. Next: [CONTROLLER_PLAN.md](../plans/input/CONTROLLER_PLAN.md) and a live presentation window.

**Probe.** A 120-flip frame budget (`BT2_FRAME_LIMIT`) is now a checked stop after a completed flip. The Timer 1 poll cap applies per wait rather than per run (the cumulative cap had stopped a 250-frame run). The startup framebuffer-edge check applies only before frame textures reuse that memory.

**Rule.** AGENTS.md gained a build-efficiency rule: iterate with narrow checks (unit, offline, subsystem, targeted builds); run full probe/ALL builds as integration gates and before commit, not after each edit.

**Measurements.** EE entries are unchanged at **642 / 633**. The run continues through **120 frames**: 225,038 calls / 224,871 returns, 1,181,339 IOP instructions, 357 logical VBlanks, 52 Timer 2 overflows, 819 VIF1 DIRECT transfers, 236 sprites.

**Verification.**
- **Builds and tests:** serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, and Python suites (**178 capture**, **63 recompilation**, **13 IOP**, 3 Ghidra).
- **Comparisons:** **27 normal-startup comparisons** (including `verify_vblank_wake` at 357 VBlanks) and six phase-matched ones.
- **Diagnostic:** the sound-worker diagnostic is unchanged (302/295).
- **Visual:** the rendered frame was inspected visually.

No window, framebuffer equivalence with an original GS dump, audio or VU execution is claimed.


## 2026-10-08 — live window, IOP VBlank and an emulated DualShock 2

**Live window** ([LIVE_WINDOW_PLAN.md](../plans/graphics/LIVE_WINDOW_PLAN.md)).
- **Decoding:** a host-side decoder turns the DISPFB/DISPLAY framebuffer of the owned GS memory into BGRA (unit-tested).
- **Presentation:** a plain Win32 window in the entry probe presents every library flip, paced to about 59.94 Hz when faster than real time.
- **Scaling:** integer by default, or stretch (`BT2_VIEWER_SCALE=stretch`). 16:9 is recorded as a later, requested option.
- **Run mode:** `run_startup_probe.py --viewer` has no frame budget and keeps the last frame shown after a stop.
- **Speed:** the Debug build runs about 12–13 fps. The user confirmed the window works.

**Root cause of the controller-missing screen.**
- **Evidence:** the startup pad poll already answered as a connected digital pad. DS2U_D's port 1 worker then waits on the IOP VBlank event flag, but the native IOP created that flag and never set it.
- **IOP VBlank:** start and end edges now follow the public vblank module (set START|VBLANK then clear START/NON_VBLANK; set END|NON_VBLANK then clear VBLANK/END). Waiters are readied in the handler and run after it returns (unit-tested).
- **ReferEventFlagStatus:** added (kernel error codes from the reference header).
- **Preemption:** SIO2MAN's worker preemption is generalized to any lower-priority caller.

**DualShock 2 model** (`runtime/iop/dualshock2.h`, unit-tested). An authored pad on SIO2 port 1:
- **Commands:** 0x42 poll (truncated replies for short polls), 0x43 config, 0x44 mode, 0x45–0x47/0x4C queries, 0x4D motor map, 0x4F response mask, 0x40/0x41.
- **Behaviour:** neutral input; starts digital, as the captured startup reply shows.
- **Port timing:** the SIO2 pad path admits the three observed port timing pairs. Port 2 stays absent.

**Follow-on IOP work driven by VBlank.**
- **DBCMAN pad DMA:** to the game's registered socket buffers (`sceSifSetDma`/`sceSifSetDmaIntr`, completion callback in interrupt context). `iSignalSema` with no waiter is generalized.
- **CRI stream returns:** CRI_ADXI's server returns its two registered stream buffers to the EE, the reverse of the verified EE→IOP copies.
- **Thread start:** the register-context area at the stack top is now zeroed, as the kernel does. SIO2D's port scan reads an uninitialized local there; with the 0xFF fill it computed slot −1 and wrote a mode ID into its own driver table. Found with the new `BT2_IOP_WATCH` watchpoint.
- **Initial SP:** kept at top − 0x20, confirmed by the original SOUNDS server descriptor address (…0x90).

**DBCMAN frame requests.** The main client (`0x800013xx`: socket attach `0x80001303`, device command `0x8000131A`) runs through the original handler.

**Generation fixes.**
- **Tail jumps:** a J from overlay code into the main executable was emitted as an inline DirectJump followed by fall-through, which overwrote the callee's return. `prepare_runtime.py` now routes it as a tail jump (unit-tested).
- **Switch proof:** `prepare_map.py` accepts a BEQ-guarded switch whose delay slot leaves the index intact (unit-tested).
- **Overlay jumps:** overlay padding proofs accept tail-jump targets in the main executable's .text (unit-tested).
- **Map size:** the overlay map grows to 456 entries.

**Result.** With the controller emulated, the game leaves the controller-missing screen. It runs 27 frames into the next stage (black frames), issuing DBCMAN socket/device requests and controller-port polls, then starts a memory card check. Normal startup advanced from 642/633 to **682 entered / 672 completed** (133,549 calls / 133,468 returns, 1,096,021 IOP instructions, 150 VBlanks). Next boundary: a frame-phase MCSERV query.

**Verification.**
- **Builds and tests:** serialized entry/ALL builds, **17/17 CTests**, and Python suites (**178 capture**, **66 recompilation**, **13 IOP**, 3 Ghidra).
- **Comparisons:** **25 normal-startup comparisons**. `verify_dbc_dma` now checks the EE destination snapshot at transfer time, since the pad library later updates that block. Ten phase-matched comparisons pass, and `verify_mc_rpc`/`verify_sound_bank` join the frame-end phase list.
- **Diagnostic:** the sound-worker diagnostic is unchanged (302/295).

No audio, real controller input, memory card contents or VU execution is claimed.

## 2026-10-08 — frame-phase memory card queries, SPU2 envelopes and GS triangles (unverified)

Committed at the user's request with final testing pending. The verified checkpoint stays at 682/672 (`66d9745`).

**Memory card.**
- **Frame-phase queries:** the game's per-frame GetInfo (port 0, slot 0, type/free/format) runs through the original MCSERV/MCMAN handlers with absent cards.
- **Repeated sequence:** MCMAN keeps the card type "no card", so every detection repeats the PS2 then PS1 probe sequence (public `mcman_detectcard`). The card path now checks its counters per query.
- **Fixes:**
  - **RPC worker:** the borrowed worker's wait fields are restored after the handler returns.
  - **Pending alarm:** the last timeout alarm of a query fires at the next field, since logical IOP time now advances per VBlank.
  - **SIO2 restart:** SIO2MAN's `ctrl|0xC` write (the control register reads back `0x3BD`) restarts over the previous query's consumed reply.
- **New capture:** `rpc-mc-frame-callback` halts at the first frame-phase end callback. It selects that callback by the GetInfo type flag, because a call counter in the scratch block was clobbered by the game. The original shows reply −11, type 0, free 0. `verify_mc_frame_query.py` matched an intermediate native report.
- **Timeouts:** captures that time out now keep `ee-ram-timeout.bin`.

**Sound driver.** The SPU2 envelope is modelled from the public ADSR register layout, PCSX2-style:
- ENVX reads return the level.
- A finished release stops the voice.
- Volume, pitch, ADSR, start/loop address and routing writes take any value, and pitch, ADSR and loop address apply to a playing voice.

MODHSYN's `GetSystemTime` and `SysClock2USec` come from logical IOP time. SB/SH stores pass only the stored bits to hardware handlers.

**GS.** Triangles, strips and fans:
- top-left fill rule;
- flat or Gouraud color;
- affine FST UVs and Z;
- shared validation and per-pixel shading with sprites.

Unit tests cover single coverage of a strip quad and a fan quad, Gouraud interpolation, flat color and XYZ3.

**Probe and map.**
- **Probe budget:** the EE runaway budget is now per frame.
- **Switch tables:** `prepare_map.py` decodes switch-table registers and accepts a store in the guard delay slot (both unit-tested).

**Exploratory result.** **771 entered / 762 completed** over **105 frames** (328,838 calls, 1,992,980 IOP instructions, 228 VBlanks, 156 triangles), stopping at the unselected `FUN_0026CD98`. This is not a verified checkpoint.

**Verification.**
- **Passed:** ALL/entry build, 17/17 CTests, 179 capture and 67 recompilation Python tests, the IOP unit/service/bridge tests and the GS command tests.
- **Pending:** IOP and Ghidra suites, the final probe runs, the capture comparisons and the PNG render.

## 2026-10-08 — build times: /MP, standard-header PCH and runtime header split

Measured on a 10-core / 20-thread i9-10900K (Debug, MSBuild, serialized runs):

| Change | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Header reaching every generated unit (`gs_command_sink.h`, `ps2_runtime.h`) | 1,045 s (779 units) | 35 s (783 units) |
| Clean entry-probe build | not measured separately (≥ the 1,045 s header rebuild) | 40 s (804 objects) |
| GS rendering edit | 1,045 s (779 units) | 2 s (1 unit) |
| Syscall dispatcher / memory-mapped I/O edit (was in `ps2_runtime.h`) | ~1,045 s (779 units) | 3 s (1 unit) |
| IOP header (`native_runtime.h`) | 43 s (20 units) | 9 s (20 units) |
| `entry_probe.cpp` | 7 s | 5 s |

Steps, each measured on the header rebuild:
1. **`/MP`:** every MSVC executable compiles its sources in parallel; MSBuild had compiled the entry probe's 798 units one at a time. 1,045 → 119 s.
2. **PCH:** the entry probe precompiles stable standard headers only (no project or generated headers). 119 → 70 s.
3. **`gs_command_sink.h`:** split into declarations plus `gs_command_sink.cpp`.
4. **`ps2_runtime.h`:** 2,435 → 745 lines. Its 27 large member bodies moved into focused files:
   - `ps2_runtime_syscalls.cpp`: the syscall and RPC dispatcher;
   - `ps2_runtime_memory.cpp`: guest memory and memory-mapped I/O;
   - `ps2_runtime_services.cpp`: the service handlers;
   - `ps2_runtime_dispatch.cpp`: call dispatch and kernel binding.
5. **Result:** the smaller header also brings full rebuilds down to 35 s.

Shared runtime sources are listed once in CMake (`BT2_PROBE_RUNTIME_SOURCES`). Unity builds are not used, and generated units stay separate. Peak memory with 20 parallel compilers is about 23 GB.

**No behavior change.**
- **Text check:** the moved bodies are verbatim. Only signature lines differ, now qualified, with default arguments kept on the declarations.
- **A/B run:** the original headers and the refactored files gave identical results on the same input. All 287 report fields match, and the EE RAM, IOP RAM, GS memory and GIF dumps are byte-identical.

The full gate then passed, except `verify_mc_sio2_flow.py`, which still has single-query expectations from the previous unverified pass. AGENTS.md now also has a code-organization rule: focused files with clear responsibilities.

- **Source lists:** `BT2_EE_RUNTIME_SOURCES`, `BT2_IOP_RUNTIME_SOURCES`, `BT2_IOP_SERVICE_SOURCES` and `BT2_EE_IOP_BRIDGE_SOURCES`, plus the generated module list, which had been repeated four times.

Targets, tests and options are unchanged.

**No behavior change.** The rebuilt probe was compared with the pre-refactor binary on the same inputs. The normal path matches on all 287 report fields, with byte-identical EE, IOP and GS dumps. The sound-worker path is identical too, at 302/295. The full gate then ran on the reorganized code:
- **Build and tests:** ALL/entry builds and **17/17 CTests**.
- **Python suites:** 179 capture, 67 recompilation, 13 IOP and 3 Ghidra tests.
- **Comparisons:** **26 normal comparisons**, where the remaining failures are exactly the known phase-scoped and argument cases, and **8 phase-matched** comparisons.
- **PNG render:** inspected visually.

**Cleanup.** Removed the following stale local residue (all ignored):
- a 296 MB archived build tree;
- an abandoned trial build;
- a removed target's intermediate directory;
- 48 generator `previous-output-*` backups;
- old A/B dumps and stale GIF mismatch dumps;
- about 600 logs.

**New rules.** The README has an architecture section. AGENTS.md has a rule for build artifacts after a pass: keep only the host build `build/` and the final probe build `build/probe/`, and delete other pass residue. The progress checklist now includes it.

## 2026-10-08 — past the no-card prompt; 6× faster selection loop

**Progress: 772 / 763 → 801 / 791, 106 → 649 frames.** The next boundary is a frame-phase GS reset (`0x30FE44`), now a checked boundary of its own (`frame_gs_reset`). It needs its own reset model and original capture comparison in a separate pass, apart from the GS performance work. The changes that got there:
- **PSMT8 textures:** the no-card dialog uses 8-bit indexed textures with a 256-entry CT32 CLUT. The PSMT8 byte layout and the CLUT entry order are written as bit placement. Both were checked offline against the reference tables, and unit-tested.
- **Timer 1 wrap:** COUNT is 16 bits and wraps silently in MODE 3 (no overflow interrupt, as in the reference). Long frames used to stop the run with "overflow is not modelled".
- **Scripted input:** with no card, the game asks "No memory card (PS2) inserted… Continue anyway?". A deterministic DualShock 2 script (`pad_script.h`; `run_startup_probe.py --pad-script`, default `400:left,408:-,420:cross,428:-`) answers Yes. Pressed buttons report full pressure in DualShock 2 mode, in the public byte order. The probe's default frame budget rose from 120 to 1,000 flips.
- **Selections:** 29 new entries, including overlay entries.

**Selection loop: about 130 s (Debug) or 32 s (first Release run) → about 20 s per iteration** at the same game point. Each change kept output byte-identical:

| Stage | Before | After | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Probe run | 112 s Debug / 17.7 s Release | 12.8 s | Release configuration (`--config Release`); GS rasterizer optimizations |
| Build per selection | 5.9 s | 2.5 s | Selected entries bound in their own file, declarations only for them, one table instead of 712 inlined calls |
| Python | 8.0 s | 3.2 s | Generated-file digests cached by size/modification time (with a test that a later edit is still caught); unchanged prepared sources and the guest image keep their timestamps; select and prepare share one process |

The GS work came from measurements: a timer showed the GS taking 92% of the run, and a sampling profile ranked `plot`, `texel`, blending and `sample`. The rasterizer changes:
- **Rollback:** copy-on-write VRAM pages replace the full 8 MB copy per transaction.
- **Sprites:** UVs computed per column and per row.
- **Triangles:** incremental edge functions and exact floor quotient/remainder interpolation, with a guarded fallback.
- **CLUT loads:** entry offsets precomputed.
- **Per primitive:** texture fields decoded once, and straight-line blending.

`expand_startup.py --callees N` can also bind static callees of a reached entry. Measured, it rarely helps here: the game reaches new code through tables and overlays. A map-gap iteration still regenerates and rebuilds everything (about 2 minutes) because the generated declaration header changes.

**Capture folder cleanup:** `work/pcsx2-capture` went from 43 GB to 11 GB. Every capture held its own 130 MB copy of the emulator package, which the capture tool recreates and no tool reads. Ten capture folders that no tool or document referenced were also removed. Capture use was traced during a full verifier run; the same 27 normal and 8 phase comparisons pass afterwards.

**Rules and docs:**
- AGENTS.md has the user's artifact rule, verbatim: keep intentional build outputs and required diagnostics, and delete disposable residue after verifying it is unused.
- Selection loops run in Release.
- The README architecture section is now a diagram and a short table.

## 2026-10-08 — frame-phase GS reset

**Scope:** only the blocker left by the previous pass.

**Implementation.** After the no-card prompt, `0x24B800` calls the GS library reset `ResetGraph(0, 1, 2, 0)` again, for the next scene. A frame-phase reset sequence now handles it:
- **Steps:** CSR reset, CSR revision read, PutIMR `0xFF00`, VSync handler removal and SetGsCrt (interlace, NTSC, field mode).
- **Gating:** the sequence is separate from the phase-locked startup model. Each step is gated on the frame phase and its program counter.
- **VSync handler:** the library's re-registration of handler `0x1006D8` is allowed once after that removal.

The probe report gains `frame_gs_resets`, `frame_gs_crt_request` and `frame_vsync_handler`.

**Original comparison.** New capture point `rpc-frame-gs-reset-after` halts at the reset's return (`0x24B81C`). The game reaches that point only after the prompt, so the capture tool got an interactive mode. `--interactive-pad` shows the PCSX2 window with its automatic renderer and binds keyboard, SDL and XInput (Xbox) controls; the user pressed Left, then Cross. `verify_frame_gs_reset.py` compares the GS library state block (`0x368090`) with the native phase stop `BT2_PHASE_STOP=24b81c`. The block is byte-identical: interlace 1, NTSC, field mode, GS revision `0x1B`, VSync handler cleared. `verify_vblank_wake.py` now counts one more handler registration per frame reset.

**Result.** 801 / 791 → **804 / 793**, 650 frames. The run passes the reset and stops at the unselected function `FUN_002B7938`; the last frame is the black scene transition.

**Gate:**
- 17/17 CTests;
- 180 capture, 68 recompilation, 13 IOP and 3 Ghidra Python tests;
- 27 normal and nine phase-matched comparisons;
- the sound-worker diagnostic, at 302/295.

## 2026-10-08 — IPU setup, scratchpad, frame-phase threads (toward the opening movie)

**Scope:** continue the selection loop after the frame-phase GS reset, with PS2Recomp's `ps2xRuntime` added as an architectural reference (PS2SDK and PCSX2 remain the behavioral references).

**Implementation:**
- **IPU setup** (`runtime/ee/ipu_device.*`): BCLR, SETIQ, SETVQ and SETTH, CTRL reset, decode parameters, and the DMAC enable registers. No decoding; any other command, and IPU DMA starts, are refused. `ipu-setup` CTest.
- **Scratchpad RAM:** a separate 16 KiB buffer at 0x70000000, checked by every accessor before main RAM. Scratchpad DMA (channels 8/9) completes synchronously in normal mode. Chain, interleave and wrapping transfers are refused (`stopped_dma_channels.h`). The game saves the whole scratchpad before the movie.
- **Uncached alias** extended to 16/64/128-bit accesses; **Timer 1** counts BUSCLK/256 in logical time; CreateSema accepts frame-phase attribute residue; CRI stream pairs are no longer capped at 39.
- **Threads** (`runtime/ee/ee_thread_scheduler.cpp`, moved out of the sound dispatch file, with `ee_execution_owner.h`):
  - a ResumeThread, WakeupThread or ChangeThreadPriority from main that readies a worker above main now runs that worker until it parks, instead of stopping;
  - main's own SleepThread waits: ready workers of any priority run, and the idle CPU takes one logical VBlank per field, up to 600;
  - wakeups of main from a worker or an interrupt end the sleep, or are banked;
  - workers read main's status as WAIT while it sleeps, READY otherwise.

  The game's frame-pacing loop now runs: worker 7 serves the callback table, main sleeps at frame end, and worker 4 wakes it.
- **GS CSR on main in the frame phase:** returns revision 0x1B, the latched status and the modelled FIELD.
- **Map tooling:** switch-case holes in noncontiguous Ghidra bodies are accepted when proven; tail-call targets after alignment NOPs are separated.
- **Viewer flicker fixed:** integer scaling fills only the borders.
- **README credits** now list PS2Recomp, BT3-Recomp, Chronicle, PS2SDK, PCSX2, Play!, Ghidra and the toolchain libraries.

**Result.** 804 / 793 → **1254 / 1243**, 650 frames, 1,034 logical VBlanks. The run stops at a GIF DMA channel 2 TADR access (`0x1000A030`, pc `0x312084`). This is likely the first chain-mode GIF transfer of the movie scene.

**User notes on game flow:** after the credits the opening movie (FMV) plays, then the title screen waits for Start. Without Start, the menu enters an attract-mode CPU-vs-CPU fight, a valuable no-input target. Movie playback needs real IPU MPEG decoding (a separate plan).

**Gate:** 18/18 CTests; 180 capture, 69 recompilation, 13 IOP and 3 Ghidra Python tests; 23 normal and nine phase-matched comparisons; the sound-worker diagnostic is unchanged at 302/295. `startup_workers`, `ee_main_status` and `cri_second_channel` describe startup worker state that frame-phase scheduling now changes later, so they are checked at the phase stops, where they pass. The GIF chain stop is an accepted entry-probe boundary, so the normal run writes a complete report.
