## 2026-10-11 — direction change: minimal native layer

**Decision (user, 2026-10-11):** keep the recompiled EE code as the base, replace the emulated IOP with native RPC services (disc, files, controller, memory card, sound, CRI ADX), and render on the GPU through paraLLEl-GS. No full decompilation; individual functions may be replaced by hand-written C++ where it pays off. The opening movie (CRI Sofdec) gets a native playback plan instead of IPU emulation.

**Evidence:** [Tenkaichi3Decomp](../reference/REFERENCE_ARCHITECTURE.md#tenkaichi3decomp-native-port-reference), a native PC port of BT3 from the same engine lineage, replaces `SOUNDS.IRX` with native audio, stubs kernel/CD/file/card services, and renders the game's GS packets on the GPU; it is not an emulator. Our measured startup run spends about 75% of its time in the CPU GS rasterizer and executes about 10.9 million IOP instructions, both of which the new direction removes.

**Docs:** AGENTS.md was compacted around the new goal; the implementation plan, PROG.md, the README and this handoff now follow the native-layer plan. Credits dropped Chronicle, Play! and the toolchain-library line (licenses remain in `LEGAL.md`) and added Tenkaichi3Decomp. No code changed.


## 2026-10-11 — native IOP services; emulated IOP removed

**Change:** every reached IOP service now runs as native host code under `runtime/services/` (SIF RPC layer, disc, FILEIO, IOP heap and uploads, LOADFILE, controller, memory card, sound driver, SOUNDS, CRI). `runtime/iop/` (IOP runtime, providers, module translation, EE bridge), its tests, the IOP generators in `tools/iop` and `config/iop_layout.json` are deleted; the ISO readers moved to `runtime/services/devices/`. [Plan](../plans/iop/NATIVE_SERVICES_PLAN.md).

**Evidence:** A/B against the emulated path: same stop (1254 / 1243, 650 frames, `0x1000A030` at pc `0x312084`), identical EE memory except stale IOP bytes at `0x3A7488`–`0x3A7536` (card info) and `0x3AC124`–`0x3AC1A0` (FILEIO completion payload). Release run time about 19.9 s → 19.1 s (about 10.9 million IOP instructions no longer run; the CPU GS rasterizer dominates).

**Verification:** Debug build, 15/15 CTests (new `bt2-native-services-tests`), Python suites 177 capture / 69 recompilation / 11 IOP inspection / 3 Ghidra. Original comparisons unchanged: 23 full run, 12 at phase stops, 4 failing as before; the 7 own-input capture checks were not rerun.

**Retired comparisons:** IOP-internal checks in `verify_cri_second_channel.py` (IOP-side pair objects and slot fields) and the `native_iop_error` / IOP-runtime report fields; the IOP module probe and its tests. Reason: they checked emulated-IOP state that no longer exists.

## 2026-10-11 — paraLLEl-GS integrated in mirror mode

**Change:** [GPU rendering plan](../plans/graphics/GPU_RENDERING_PLAN.md); paraLLEl-GS pinned (`config/parallel_gs.lock.json`, `tools/graphics/gpu/fetch_parallel_gs.py`) and built through CMake behind `BT2_GPU_GS`; `runtime/gs/gpu/gpu_gs_backend.*` and `gpu_gs_mirror.*`; the CPU sink reports accepted GIF data through `on_accepted`; new `bt2-gpu-gs-tests` (a sprite renders identically on both backends).

**Evidence:** Release run with `BT2_GPU_GS=mirror` (about 32 s with both renderers): same stop (1254 / 1243, 650 frames); frames 1–29 identical in every VRAM word the CPU model knows; from frame 30, 15 to a few hundred words per frame differ by one step in one channel (first: word `0x1DC0D`, CPU `0x100000`, GPU `0x110000`). Consistent with interpolation rounding; undecided until a PCSX2 GS dump is available.

**Stop:** deciding the differences needs a PCSX2 GS dump of the startup frames, not available in this session.

**Presentation and GPU-only (same day):** privileged registers and SMODE1 (from the game's `SetGsCrt` request) feed paraLLEl-GS's scanout, shown in the viewer or saved per flip; GPU flips 120 and 400 match the CPU frames visually. `BT2_GPU_GS=on` keeps the CPU model as validator only (`rasterize=false`, counters kept) and flushes the GPU each flip: same stop and counters, flip 400 pixel-identical to mirror mode, Release startup run **2.3 s instead of 19.1 s**. A first GPU-only attempt without per-flip flushes presented stale frames; fixed.

**Layout (same day):** CPU and GPU renderer code split into `runtime/gs/cpu/` (GS model, `cpu_display.h` decoder split from `host/display_frame.h`) and `runtime/gs/gpu/`; tools into `tools/graphics/cpu/` (`render_gs_frame.py`) and `tools/graphics/gpu/` (`fetch_parallel_gs.py`). `run_startup_probe.py --gs` selects the renderer and defaults to GPU-only when the build has `BT2_GPU_GS`; a probe without it rejects `BT2_GPU_GS`.

## 2026-10-11 — GIF DMA chain mode

**Change:** `runtime/ee/dma_chain.*` walks EE DMA source chains (CNT, NEXT, REF, REFS, REFE, END; CALL/RET and tag IRQ refused) for VIF1 and, new, frame-phase GIF channel 2 (TADR, CHCR `0x105` with the read-only TAG field carried back). GIF chain data is joined and sent as one GS transaction. The CPU GS model now names each refused drawing feature and supports the depth tests NEVER/GEQUAL/GREATER (refusing reads of unwritten depth). Four newly reached functions were selected (`FUN_002B8398`, `FUN_002BD3C8`, `FUN_002BB530`, and the map gap `FUN_002BCC08`, discovered with Ghidra; 9,494 mapped). Tests: `bt2-dma-chain-tests`, depth tests in `bt2-gs-command-tests`.

**Evidence:** the run passes the old stop (one chain transfer) and reaches 1,259 entered / 1,249 completed functions, 650 frames, then stops at the CRI service's checked boundary: the first ADX stream commands (8 and 5 on object `0x1B5E2C`), now printed when refused. [Sofdec plan](../plans/video/SOFDEC_PLAN.md) written; PCSX2 and the capture BIOS are available locally for the reply capture.

## 2026-10-12 — CRI stream replies; the movie reaches the IPU

**Capture:** new `capture_ee.py` point `rpc-cri-stream-reply` with unattended input: a hook in the pad reader (`0x28BEA4`) presses LEFT then CROSS in cycles to answer the absent-card prompt, an upload hook flags the first command-carrying upload of CRI channel `0x35FD00`, and the trap halts at the pump's next reply-handler call (`0x2FCEE8`). Flag and counter live inside the authored segment (game RAM near the marker is in use). Result: the IOP returns the buffer with the command count cleared and the records (8 and 5) unchanged.

**Change:** `runtime/services/cri_stream.h` consumes stream commands (8 and 5; others refused) and the CRI service echoes the buffer that way; counter `native_cri_commands`; test in `bt2-native-services-tests`. The selection loop compiled 380 newly reached functions (with static callees; five map gaps discovered; 9,499 mapped).

**Evidence:** the run reaches 1,590 entered functions, 650 frames, and stops at the movie's first toIPU DMA (`0x1000B400`, pc `0x2CB384`): the game's Sofdec library drives the IPU, so the movie needs native IPU decoding.

**Boundary handling (same day):** the probe accepted frame-phase memory stops from a list of guest PCs; it now treats any refused hardware-register access in the frame phase (EE `0x10000000`–`0x1000FFFF`, GS privileged `0x12000000`–`0x12001FFF`) as the checked boundary, named by its address, while refused RAM accesses stay failures. `verify_cri_autodma.py` accepts the game's own later cache scans (whole 128-line passes). Gate: 17/17 CTests; 23 comparisons on the full run, 12 at phase stops.

## 2026-10-12 — native IPU input; the movie parses its header

**Change:** [IPU plan](../plans/video/IPU_PLAN.md). `runtime/ee/ipu_input.*`: toIPU channel 4 in normal and source-chain mode keeps the input FIFO full (filled on start and after each consumption), the bit buffer and IPU_BP/IPU_TOP, BCLR/SETIQ/SETVQ on the same input, FDEC, and commands that wait for input with BUSY set; clearing STR stops a running transfer with its registers and FIFO kept. 64-bit loads of IPU registers return BUSY in bit 63. Map: the candidate export keeps functions whose holes no other function owns, and the switch prover accepts calls in case blocks and a last block falling through (9,504 mapped). Runtime macros gain the HI:LO accumulator helpers for MADD.

**Evidence:** the movie starts its toIPU chain (a ring of 2 KB REF chunks of an MPEG stream, 512×448 at 29.97 Hz), stops and restarts it, and parses the sequence header with FDEC (`000001B3`, then 32, 32 and 30 bits). Its decoder thread then waits in the loop at `0x31A3C8` without a further command; the run reaches 1,602 entered / 1,586 completed functions and ends at the execution budget.

## 2026-10-12 — native IPU decoding; the movie presents its first pictures

**Cause of the wait:** the generator emits 32-bit sign tests for BLTZ/BGEZ/BLEZ/BGTZ; the R5900 compares 64 bits. The movie's `bgezl` on a 64-bit IPU_CMD read took bit 31 of the FDEC result for BUSY. `prepare_runtime.py` widens all such branches (491 in the prepared sources; tooling test).

**Change:** [IPU plan](../plans/video/IPU_PLAN.md#progress-2026-10-12-later). VDEC (`runtime/ee/ipu_vlc.*`), BDEC (`ipu_block.*`: tables B-9 and B-12 to B-15 checked at compile time, inverse quantisation, inverse DCT), CSC to RGB32 (`ipu_csc.*`), the output FIFO and fromIPU DMA, bit-level SETIQ/SETVQ, IPU_CTRL status fields; toSPR source-chain DMA; MMI helpers PADDH, PMAXH, PMINH, PPACB, PEXTLB, PEXTUB, QFSRV and the trapping add; GIF IMAGE data continuing across transfers; CRI stream commands 2, 4, 9 and `0x100` from four new PCSX2 captures (capture triggers by command count, first code, channel and reply delay). A frame is now counted at the library flip or at the VBLANK ending a field whose display update had none (the movie's per-field update); the runaway guard applies per field and time without a frame is bounded (3,600 fields). The probe report gains IPU counters.

**Evidence:** the run reaches 1,829 entered / 1,819 completed functions (9,517 mapped, 1,964 + 12 compiled) and the frame budget of 1,000 frames, 350 of them movie fields. The movie decodes an I, a P and a B picture (1,088 coded macroblocks, 2,688 converted) and uploads them to the displayed framebuffer; CPU and GPU renderers show the same picture. It then waits for audio playback progress, which the native CRI service does not report: the next blocker ([Sofdec plan](../plans/video/SOFDEC_PLAN.md#progress-2026-10-12-later)).

## 2026-10-12 — native ADX audio flow; both movies play to the end

**Finding:** the buffer the IOP returns on a CRI stream channel carries the IOP's own messages (count in word 0, records {0, EE object, address, bytes}). A PCSX2 log of both channels (`capture_ee.py --cri-stream-log`) shows the player reporting played PCM in `0x400`-byte blocks per source at 48 kHz, a full report of the remaining data at a stop, and the player's destruction and re-creation between the movies.

**Change:** `runtime/services/cri_audio.h` models sources, players and the played-data reports on a logical 48 kHz clock; RPC `0x409` destroys a player and frees its handle for reuse; the refused request of any native service now names itself. Kernel and hardware: admitted main may delete its newest semaphore in any state; Timer 1 mode writes may carry the flag-acknowledge bits; a main thread that executes 100,000 guest calls without an idle point receives a VBLANK (general replacement for further address-specific poll hooks); MMI PCGTH. Capture tool: stream log of all channels, `read_cri_stream_log.py`.

**Evidence:** the long Release run (9,000-frame budget) plays the logo movie (151 pictures) and the opening movie (3,236 pictures), 7,902 presented frames, and stops at SOUNDS function `0x50` (reply to be captured). The logo's first picture, a green leader frame the user did not expect, equals ffmpeg's decode of the same file; mid-movie frames show the expected logo and opening. Gate: 17/17 CTests, the same original comparisons as before (23 full-run, nine at phase stops); the gate run reaches 2,007 entered / 1,998 completed at its 1,000-frame budget.

## 2026-10-12 — title screen; every mapped function compiled; the 3D pipeline is next

**Reached:** with scripted Start presses the movies are skipped (seconds per run), the title screen renders with its options (confirmed by the user) and New Game leads into the game's 3D setup. The long Release run reaches 3,198 entered / 3,189 completed functions and stops at the first VIF1 display list with tag transfer (CHCR `0x145`, pc `0x28B3E8`): [3D pipeline plan](../plans/graphics/VU1_PIPELINE_PLAN.md).

**Services:** SOUNDS replies from a PCSX2 call log of 40 calls (`capture_ee.py --sounds-log`): every reply is the status block, whose word 5 counts loaded banks (`0x20` +1, `0x30` -1); `0x60`, `0x80`, `0x100` are answered the same way by inference. CRI: the 24 kHz music stream (rate command 4, command 3, half-size blocks, logged), a player destroyed while playing (skipped movie). A refused native request names itself.

**EE:** VU0 macro mode (float registers with VF0 constant, ACC, Q, R, integer registers, masked VADD/VSUB/VMUL, VSQI/VLQD/VLQI/VSQD on VU0 data memory executed from the instruction word, the status flags of the clip tests, VPU_STAT idle in the frame phase from any thread); PEXTLW/PEXTUW (all 21 helper forms the generated game uses now exist); logical CPU time for a busy main thread also per loop iteration, with interrupts enabled; DMA CALL/RET tags; Timer 1 flag-acknowledge bits; DeleteSema of main's newest semaphore.

**Recompilation pipeline:** recursive self-calls go through the dispatcher; prepared sources no longer include the generator's declaration headers (a map change recompiled all ~9,500 files: about 14 minutes; now about 1 minute); `expand_startup.py --all` binds every mapped function and `--neighbours` nearby ones; `DiscoverBT2Prologues.java` (`analyze_game.py --discover-prologues`) creates, in one pass, the functions the automatic analysis missed (1,081 found: code after a completed function that is outside every function's extent); tail-call splitting also recognizes a tail jump as a function end; the jump-table proofs accept a bound in a branch delay slot or separated from its guard by register saves, FPU stores in the delay slot, several tables per function, cases that fall through, conditional branches and tail jumps inside case blocks, and padding holes next to case holes. The map grew from 9,547 to 10,650 functions.

**Gate:** 17/17 CTests, the same original comparisons as before; the gate run reaches 2,007 entered / 1,998 completed at its 1,000-frame budget.

## 2026-10-13 — build and gate time

No change in behaviour: the gate run still reaches 2,007 entered / 1,998 completed with the same counters, and the long Release run 3,198 / 3,189 in the same time (about 30 s).

**Header split:** generated code now includes only `ps2_runtime.h`, which holds the CPU context and `PS2Runtime`, the 31 entry points generated code calls. The full runtime is `EeRuntime` in `ee_runtime.h`, derived from it; `guest_interface.cpp` forwards the entry points. A runtime header edit no longer recompiles the generated files.

**Build system:** the probe builds with Ninja through `tools/build/msvc.py` (the Visual Studio generator spent about 55 s per build in its link task for 10,600 objects, while the linker itself needs 3 to 5 s); Release links incrementally; the handwritten runtime is one library instead of being recompiled for each of 11 targets; the generated code is an object library with its own small precompiled header.

**Measured (10-core / 20-thread machine):**

| Build | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Runtime header edit (Release) | about 14 min (full rebuild) | 16 s |
| One runtime or generated file | 57 to 70 s | 12 s |
| Nothing changed | 29 s | 5 s |
| Full Release rebuild | about 14 min | about 6 min |

**Gate:** `tools/gate/run_gate.py` runs CTest, the Python suites and the six Debug probe runs concurrently (`run_startup_probe.py --report` gives each its own report; the CTest entry probe no longer overwrites `entry-stop.json`), then the comparisons. Debug compiles the generated code and the runtime's hot sources (guest memory, guest calls, CPU renderer, movie decoding, FPU) optimized: the Debug full run takes 70 s instead of 265 s. The gate takes about 80 s after the build; its probe runs alone took about 325 s one after another.

## 2026-10-13 — VIF1 display lists and VU1; the demo fights render in 3D

**Evidence first:** the first 3D display list (1,029 DMA tags, 836,000 words) uses MPG, BASE, OFFSET, UNPACK V4-32 (plain write cycle, with and without TOPS), MSCALF, MSCNT, FLUSHE and DIRECT, and uploads five microprograms (101 to 451 instructions). Their instruction set is small: LQ/SQ/ILW/ISW forms, integer arithmetic, MOVE/MR32, the ADD/SUB/MUL/MADD families with ACC, Q and I, ABS, ITOF0, FTOI0/4, CLIP with FCAND/FCOR/FCGET, DIV/WAITQ, branches, XTOP and XGKICK. The flag instructions are followed directly by their branch (`FCOR` / `IBNE vi1`), so that pair reads the new value.

**3D pipeline** (`runtime/vu/`, [plan](../plans/graphics/VU1_PIPELINE_PLAN.md)): `vif1.*` interprets the VIFcodes of a transfer (checked as a whole first, so a code that is not modelled stops before any effect); `vu1.*` holds VU1 memory and the microprogram interpreter (upper and lower instruction per cycle with the upper write winning, Q after 7 cycles, clip flags after 4, branch delay slots, E bit, round toward zero). XGKICK packets and DIRECT data reach the GS in list order. A DIRECT may now continue behind the next DMA tag. Forms that are not reached (V2/V3 UNPACK, masking, addition modes, MSKPATH3, MAX/MINI, the MAC and status flag instructions, a branch on an integer register written by the previous non-flag instruction) are refused by name. 18th CTest: `vu1` (per code, UNPACK form and instruction).

**GS:** in GPU-only mode the CPU model no longer applies the limits of its own rasterizer (drawing state, primitive types, transfer formats, CLUT forms); it still checks packet framing and register numbers and refuses local-to-host transfers. The CPU renderer itself does not draw 3D yet.

**Two generator errors, corrected in `prepare_runtime.py`:** (1) `SQRT.S` takes its operand from `ft`; the generated code took the root of `fs` (`$f0`) at 110 of 150 sites. (2) An instruction with VF0 as destination has only its flag effects; the generated code wrote the register at 34 sites (the clip tests), after which every routine using `vf0` as (0, 0, 0, 1) computed garbage. Each showed as the same hang: a movement routine stepping through a distance of 1e11. Found with two new diagnostics: `BT2_STALL_FIELDS` and `BT2_EE_WATCH_FLOAT`.

**Jump tables:** a `JR` the generator did not resolve now continues at the function's own entry label (`route_local_indirect_jumps`, 227 sites). The map prover accepts FPU-only instructions around a switch guard, FPU condition branches and branch-target continuation blocks inside case blocks, padding after a proven switch jump, and switches whose index is reloaded from the stack frame with the bound kept in a stack slot or in a callee-saved register (each with tests). Map: 10,650 to 10,680 functions.

**Result:** the long Release run (movies skipped) reaches its 9,000-frame budget: title screen, a demo fight, the title again, a second demo fight; 4,854 entered / 4,847 completed functions; 8,903 display lists, 7.9 million microprogram starts, 4.43 billion VU1 instructions, 10.5 million XGKICK packets, no read inside a Q or clip-flag delay; 214 s. Six frames saved for inspection (`work/frames/demo-*.png`). The note of 2026-10-12 that the Start presses chose New Game was wrong: that stop was the demo fight's first display list.

**Gate:** 18/18 CTests, Python suites (85 recompilation tests), 32/32 comparisons and renders; the gate run is unchanged at 2,007 entered / 1,998 completed and 11,505,514 calls.

**The title input:** the scripted Start presses of the earlier runs did open the title's option list; what was missing was the confirm button. Start opens the list ("New Game" first), **Cross confirms**. Two dialogues follow: "Do you wish to create a save file on the memory card (PS2)?" (No preselected) and "Start a new game without saving?" (No preselected; Left and Cross choose Yes). Pad scripts count fields and snapshots count flips; the probe now prints the field of each snapshot (the two differ by about 695 at the title screen).

**Path to a battle, with scripted input:** New Game, main menu (Dragon Adventure, Ultimate Battle Z, Dragon Tournament, Dueling, Ultimate Training, Evolution Z, Item Shop, Data Center, Options, Dragon Library all scroll), Dueling, 1P VS COM, Single Battle, character selection (mode, colour, COM pick), map selection, the loading screen, then the fight: stage fly-over, intro poses, HUD, lock-on, combos. Scripted Up and Square move the fighter and attack. The user confirmed the title dialogues, the menus and character selection by inspection.

**Boundaries on the way:**
- **Overlay functions:** the first stop after New Game was a mapped overlay function that was not compiled in. `expand_startup.py --all` now binds every mapped overlay entry (473 for `DBZP.BIN`).
- **Runaway guard:** battle loading runs a worker thread for tens of millions of loop iterations within one field (it sits above main, so logical time does not advance for it). The frame-phase bound of the guard is now 400 million iterations per field; `BT2_EE_LOOP_LIMIT` overrides it.
- **One map gap** (`0x25BF98`), mapped by the discovery loop; its discovery added two functions: 10,680 to 10,682.
- **GS readback** ([plan](../plans/graphics/GS_READBACK_PLAN.md)): battle loading calls the GS library's store-image routine 12 times (258,048 quadwords). New: GsGetIMR/GsPutIMR in the frame phase, VIF1 normal-mode DMA in both directions, MSKPATH3, the FINISH event in CSR, VIF1_STAT's FDR and FQC, BUSDIR, and VIF1 FIFO reads and writes (`runtime/ee/ee_gs_readback.cpp`). The pixels come from paraLLEl-GS's own local-to-host transfer in GPU-only mode and from the CPU model's VRAM (CT32/CT24) otherwise.

**Tools:** the discovery loop of the previous pass is now `tools/ps2recomp/discovery_loop.py` (handles the main executable and the overlay program, checks receipt changes line by line, keeps only the latest previous output, writes a live log), with tests.

**Result:** the long Release run with the battle pad script reaches its 9,000-frame budget in 277 s: 4,936 entered / 4,927 completed functions, 14,334 fields, 8,903 display lists, 16.8 million microprogram starts, 5.77 billion VU1 instructions, 20.7 million XGKICK packets, and still no VU1 read inside a pipeline delay. The battle part presents about 19 frames per second of wall-clock time (one run, one scene) and one frame per two fields of game time; neither is compared with the original.

**Gate:** 18/18 CTests (new cases for the sink's download, MSKPATH3 and the EE readback sequence), Python suites (91 recompilation tests), 32/32 comparisons and renders; the gate run is unchanged at 2,007 entered / 1,998 completed and 11,505,514 calls.

## 2026-10-13 — Stream audio output (small pass)

**Evidence:** the game's CRI ADX library decodes on the EE and uploads finished PCM to IOP memory; the IOP only plays it, and the native CRI service already tracks every played block for pacing. So stream audio needs no native decoder. Sound effects go through SOUNDS and sound banks and are a separate, larger step ([plan](../plans/sound/SOUND_OUTPUT_PLAN.md)).

**Change:** `CriAudioFlow` passes on each stretch a playing player consumed (not what a stop merely reports, not a destroyed player's data). `AudioMixer` (`runtime/services/audio_mixer.h`) turns the sources into 48 kHz stereo, 800.8 samples per field, with one block of start silence per voice against the 512-sample block cadence. The probe writes the result with `BT2_AUDIO_WAV=<path>` (`runtime/host/wav_writer.h`).

**Measured:** the 7,000-frame battle run (10,334 fields) gives a 172.4 s WAV from 19.4 MB of stream PCM; 5,939 samples were missing where a voice ended or ran dry. The signal is audio-like (sample-to-sample correlation about 0.98). Not listened to and not compared with the original; which source is left and which is right is an assumption (first buffer left).

**Gate:** 18/18 CTests (new cases for the audible stretches and the mixer), Python suites, 32/32 comparisons and renders; the gate run is unchanged at 2,007 entered / 1,998 completed and 11,505,514 calls.

**After the user's listening check:** the audio is recognizable; music is too loud, voices too quiet, effects too loud. The stream commands were logged (`cri_stream_commands` in the report): pan −15 on channel 0 and +15 on channel 1, and a value of 0 in every use of command 5, so no command sets a balance; the mixer adds overlapping players at full level. The level has to come from a comparison with a PCSX2 recording (next step; the port's side of an input-free boot run is `work/audio/boot-run.wav`).

## 2026-10-13 — Stream audio compared with a PCSX2 recording (small pass)

**Reference:** a PCSX2 audio recording by the user: boot without input through the logo and opening movies into the menus (204.8 s, 48 kHz stereo). **Tool:** `tools/audio/compare_audio.py` finds the port's audio in the reference and reports offset, level ratio, waveform likeness and channel match per segment (tests in `tools/audio/`, now a gate suite).

**Result for the two movies:** constant offset (no drift), the channel balance tracks the original (correlation 0.994: first buffer left, second right), and the unscaled stream PCM was 2.53 to 2.58 times too loud at every frequency. `AudioMixer` now outputs a stream voice at 25/64; the RMS ratio is then 1.00 to 1.08 and the peaks agree. The battle run's WAV no longer clips (peak 21,315). The level is measured; where the original applies it is not known.

**Not settled:** the waveforms are alike only in short windows (coherence about 0.97, sample correlation about 0.5, alignment wandering by tens of samples), as a time-stretched recording would be; a recording without time stretching would decide it. The menu and battle audio is not compared (it needs the same input on both sides), and the user has not yet listened at the new level.

**Gate:** 18/18 CTests, Python suites (new: 3 audio comparison tests), 32/32 comparisons and renders; the gate run is unchanged at 2,007 entered / 1,998 completed and 11,505,514 calls.

## 2026-10-13 — Sound effects: a native sampler behind SOUNDS

**Evidence first** (`BT2_SOUND_LOG`, battle run): the SOUNDS requests and the banks were logged and read. Play requests (`0x40`) carry up to 15 sounds in parallel arrays: bank slot, sound number, volume, pan, pitch offset. Banks are Sony's chunked sound data (`Vagi` sample table, `Setb` timbres, `Sesq` sequences), their samples moved to sound memory by the sound driver's block transfer. All 233 sounds of the eight banks are a single note-on of one timbre with identical parameters; three samples loop. BT2 has the regular shape the BT3 port reported, so no sequencer is needed.

**Code** (`runtime/services/`): `spu_adpcm.h` (the chip's sample format), `sound_bank.h` (bank parser; a bank outside the regular shape is refused at the load), `sound_sampler.h` (2 MB sound memory, the bank stack, up to 48 voices with pitch and constant-power pan). The level is the product of the five volumes on the way and the chip's half-scale voice volume (0.244 at a full request, the BT3 port's figure). `AudioMixer::field` takes the sampler as an extra source. Looping samples and slots with flag bits stay silent and are counted; requests `0x50`, `0x100` and `0x200` are not decoded.

**Measured (7,000-frame battle run):** 278 effects played, 10 silent for their slot flag, at most 16 voices at once; the first menu sounds appear in the WAV at the fields of their requests, after silence. Not listened to yet and not compared with the original.

**Gate:** 18/18 CTests (new cases for the decoder, the bank parser and the sampler), Python suites, 32/32 comparisons and renders; the gate run is unchanged at 2,007 entered / 1,998 completed and 11,505,514 calls (4 effects play in it).

## 2026-10-13 — Sound effects: stops, looping sounds and slot volumes from the module's code

**The user's listening:** the battle run with one-shot effects (menus and a fight) sounds good and the effects are present.

**Evidence:** the game's SOUNDS module is small and unoptimized, so its request handlers were read from a local disassembly instead of being guessed from the request log. The slot byte of a play request holds the slot in its low five bits and two flags: `0x80` keeps the sound in a table of 48 so it can be stopped, `0x40` stops the kept sound of that slot and number. `0x50` fades the volume of the slots in a mask to zero or back to full, `0x200` silences the slots in a mask, `0x100` stops everything, `0x70` sets all slot volumes, `0x60` selects the output mode and `0x80` replaces a bank ([sound plan](../plans/sound/SOUND_OUTPUT_PLAN.md), Evidence).

**Code:** `sound_sampler.h` plays looping samples from their loop start, keeps and stops sounds, applies a volume per slot and silences slots; a stopped voice fades over 10 ms in place of the unmodelled release. `sound_service.cpp` routes the requests; `0x60` and `0x80`, which no run has reached, are now refused by name instead of being answered without effect.

**Measured (9,000-frame battle run, 239 s of audio):** 457 effects played, at most 16 voices at once. 9 sounds were kept and each was followed by its stop: 7 ended a playing voice, 2 came after the sound had ended. The looping sound played 3 times and was stopped each time. The run reaches the same checkpoint as before (4,936 entered / 4,927 completed). The looping sounds have not been listened to; nothing of the effects is compared with the original.

**Gate:** passed: the unit tests (new sampler cases for looping, keep and stop, slot volume and silencing), the Python suites, the probe runs and 32/32 comparisons and renders.

## 2026-10-13 — Host audio device

**Code:** `runtime/host/audio_device.h` plays each field of mixed audio through Win32 waveOut. The device's clock paces the run: while more than a tenth of a second of audio waits in the device, the writer sleeps. `run_startup_probe.py --sound` (`BT2_AUDIO_DEVICE`) turns it on, alone or with the viewer and the WAV file; without it runs are unpaced as before.

**Measured:** a standalone check queued 120 fields (2.0 s of audio) in 1.93 s. A 900-frame boot run played all 1,283 fields without running dry and waited for the device 1,301 times: boot and movies run faster than the game's speed and are held to it. Menus and the fight were not measured with the device; the fight runs below the game's speed, so gaps are expected there until the performance work. The user has not yet listened to live sound.

**Gate:** passed (unit tests, Python suites, probe runs, 32/32 comparisons and renders); gate runs do not use the device.

## 2026-10-13 — Controller support: keyboard, XInput and DualSense

**Code:** `runtime/host/host_input.h` reads the keyboard, the first connected XInput pad and a DualSense directly over HID (its own thread; USB and Bluetooth report layouts in `dualsense_report.h`). `input_mapping.h` maps keys and a neutral gamepad state (`gamepad.h`) to the game's buttons and sticks, with a small dead zone. The native controller service asks for live input once per field (`on_pad_input`) and reports changes with the pad script's field number (`on_pad_change`), so the probe can record a session as a pad script. Under live input a pad script is not loaded. `run_startup_probe.py --play [--record <file>]`.

**Launcher plan:** the default overlay combination is now the PS button on a DualSense, L3 + R3 on any gamepad and Escape on the keyboard, because Start and Select both reach the game; Start + Select remains selectable.

## 2026-10-13 — Performance: pacing fixed, VU1 arithmetic vectorized, the fight measured

The user reported the window as laggy with the graphics card at about 16% load. Measured with new per-part timers (`BT2_VIEWER_STATS`), details in the [performance plan](../plans/ee/PERFORMANCE_PLAN.md).

- **Menus and movies:** about 33 flips per second where 60 were due, because the window and the audio device both paced the run with sleeps of about 15 ms granularity. With one pacer and 1 ms sleeps the menus hold 60.
- **The fight:** 59 to 73 ms per flip, of which VU1 with its packets 36 to 50 ms. The VU1 arithmetic now runs four lanes at once with the float mode in MXCSR: 48 to 58 ms per flip, VU1 25 to 35 ms. The output is bit-identical on the 5,100-frame fight script (784,729,536 instruction pairs, packet hash `f3da74e43010e423` before and after).
- **Pictures per second in the fight:** the fight's main loop waits 400 H-BLANK counts from the top of each frame before it waits for the vertical blank, which makes every frame two fields long (30 per second). Read from the code; the user recalls 60 for the original; to be checked on the original.
- **Not done:** the fight is still slower than either budget. Next is predecoding VU1 programs.

**Gate:** passed (unit tests, Python suites, probe runs, comparisons and renders).

## 2026-10-13 — Performance: the fight from 59–73 ms to 32–38 ms per flip

Continued from the entry above ([performance plan](../plans/ee/PERFORMANCE_PLAN.md)); every step kept the fight run's instruction count, call count and VU1 output hash (`f3da74e43010e423`).

- **VU1 pairs decoded once** into a table; arithmetic, plain loads and stores and no-operations run from it: 39 to 46 ms per flip.
- **No CPU copy of texture uploads in GPU-only mode:** VIF1 from about 4.6 to about 2.9 ms, GS packets from about 7.7 to about 6 ms.
- **A sampling profiler** (`runtime/host/sample_profiler.h`, `tools/perf/profile_report.py`) showed the game-code side dominated by the runtime's own overhead: the chain of device cases in front of every word access, and two set insertions per call.
- **RAM-first word loads and stores, a small table in front of the call sets:** 32 to 38 ms per flip; the game code and services share fell from about 13 to about 8 ms.

The user saw the disk at full activity during one measurement; it was Visual Studio's background download on drive C, not the port (which had read 41 MB from drive D).

**Not done:** heavier moments still miss the 33.4 ms budget, and a 16.7 ms budget (if the original shows 60 pictures per second in fights) is far off. One scene on one machine.

**Gate:** passed (unit tests, Python suites with the new profile report tests, probe runs, 32/32 comparisons and renders).

## 2026-10-13 — Performance requirements, a clock-based pacer, the extended fight measured

**Requirements from the user:** fights a flat 30 pictures per second (the user confirmed 30 on the original in PCSX2, agreeing with the fight loop's two-field frame), menus a flat 60 without dips, never faster than the game, and all of it on a mid- to low-end PC as well ([performance plan](../plans/ee/PERFORMANCE_PLAN.md)).

- **More work removed** (output hash unchanged): the VIF1 list buffer is reused; the EE FPU helpers set rounding in MXCSR; the CPU GS model follows only A+D registers in GPU-only mode; frame timers are free unless the statistics are on. The first 20 seconds of the fight: 27 to 33 ms per flip unpaced.
- **Menus were not flat:** pacing on the sound device's queue made 15 to 20 of every 120 pictures late (the slowest 26 to 30 ms). A clock-based pacer with a high-resolution timer (`runtime/host/frame_pacer.h`) waits once per field for its deadline and follows the device's queue by adjusting the period within 1%: 0 or 1 of 120 late, slowest about 20 ms, 6 to 7.5 ms of work per picture.
- **The fight over about 100 seconds** (the user pointed out the earlier measurement ended right after the fight began): 25 to 31 ms of work per picture in most stretches, 35 to 39 ms in the heaviest, where the rate falls to 23 to 28 pictures per second. Not flat yet.
- **Tools:** the sampling profiler notes the calling function for samples in library code and has a report by source line.
- **Open:** hitches of 0.1 to 0.3 s at screen changes (loading within one field).

**Gate:** passed, after two unit tests were updated to the GPU-only model no longer counting primitives.

## 2026-10-13 — VU1 microprograms as native code

- **Census:** the extended fight run starts 14 distinct VU1 programs with 1,720 instruction pairs in all (`BT2_VU1_CENSUS`; a program is keyed by the pairs reachable from its start address, `runtime/vu/vu1_program.h`).
- **Translation:** `tools/vu1/translate_programs.py` writes one C++ function per program: frequent instructions with their operands as constants, the rest through the interpreter's own `upper` and `lower`, control flow as labels and gotos, a hand-over to the interpreter for anything outside the program. The build uses the generated file when it exists (`game/generated/vu1/`, ignored).
- **Equality:** on the fight script all 2,501,695 starts ran native code; instruction count, call count and output hash are the baseline's.
- **Measured (extended fight, viewer and sound):** every 120-flip window at 29.8 to 30.0 pictures per second; work per picture 18 to 27 ms in most stretches, 29 to 31.5 ms in the heaviest (25 to 39 ms with the interpreter); VU1 with its packets 7 to 17 ms (14 to 24.5 ms before).
- **Not flat yet:** the slowest picture of a window is 37 to 59 ms; the heaviest stretch has no margin on this machine.
- **Found on the way:** the VU1 unit tests overflowed their stack once a `Vu1` object passed 100 KB (the decoded-pair table and the lookup tables); the test executable has a larger stack now.

**Gate:** passed (unit tests with the new program-key cases, Python suites with the translator tests, probe runs, comparisons and renders).

## 2026-10-13 — Camera bug: two more generator errors

The user reported, with a picture, that the fight camera does not follow the player. The scripted run reproduced it headless (and the frames confirmed earlier had it too, so it was not caused by the speed work): for seconds at a time the camera stood close to the fighters and looked past them.

- **Followed backwards with two new diagnostics** (`BT2_EE_CALL_TRACE`, `BT2_EE_WATCH_QUADWORD`): the view matrix, the routine that builds it from a position and angles, the battle camera that produces the angles, and the C library's sine routine. The angles were right; the matrix had the sine terms with the wrong sign, a view mirrored about one axis, which shows whenever the camera looks along the far half of the circle.
- **Cause: `MFC1` was zero-extended.** The R5900 sign-extends the 32-bit pattern into the 64-bit register. The library's range reduction tests the sign of a float on its bits with a 64-bit branch, so a negative angle beyond three quarters of pi was reduced as if positive and `sinf` returned the wrong sign. The same holds for every other sign test done that way: 1,821 sites in the game and its overlay. `prepare_runtime.py` now emits the sign extension (`sign_extend_float_moves`).
- **Found on the way: the VU0 random number instructions.** The generated `VRNEXT` advanced a generator of its own invention and never wrote its destination, so the game's random number routine returned whatever the register held. `VRINIT`, `VRXOR`, `VRNEXT` and `VRGET` now run natively from the instruction word with the register's 23-bit sequence (`EeRuntime::vu0RandomInstruction`, 16 sites).
- **Effect on the game:** the camera frames both fighters throughout the scripted fight; the opponent, who had only stood and taken hits, blocks, counters and fires beams. The menus and the script's path into the fight are unchanged.
- **New baseline** (5,100 frames of `pad-fight.txt`, headless): `vu1_kick_hash` `5a4960667d4a3dff`, 724,698,589 instruction pairs, 2,140,195 starts, 39,256,326 calls; identical with every program interpreted.
- **Hand-written native VU1 programs** are the direction for VU1 (decided by the user). Prepared: the census now measures each program's cost (`cost.txt`), `tools/vu1/disassemble.py` lists a program, and the plan has the method and the order. Five of the 14 programs carry about 94% of the time.
- **Frames for the user:** `work/frames/cam/sx-a.png`, `sx-b.png` (after), `base-sheet.png` (before).

**Gate:** passed (unit tests with the random number register cases, Python suites with the new preparation and disassembler tests, probe runs, 32 comparisons and renders). A first run failed on an error in the new unit test itself, corrected.

## 2026-10-13 — Mapping in bulk instead of one stop at a time

- **The user's request:** stops at unmapped functions came one or two per discovery run; that is no way to work.
- **Why functions were missing:** the map builder refused 61 candidates in the main program and 4 in the overlay. Almost all were one of three things: a function Ghidra had merged with a small piece of code elsewhere (a shared tail it jumps to); a switch with more than 256 cases (302 in the overlay); a switch whose bound is not next to its jump.
- **Map builder:** merged candidates are taken apart at a completed transfer (`split_merged`); tables of any length; a table without a nearby bound is read by scanning, with the holes still checked; refused candidates and listed entry addresses become functions by the builder's own rules (`add_entries`), including splitting a function at an entry where the code before is complete and nothing branches across.
- **Scan:** `map_gaps.py` finds every call, jump and data pointer target that is not a function. After the changes it finds none: main 10,994 → 11,122 functions, overlay 490 → 494, no refused candidate left.
- **Loop:** `discovery_loop.py --map-all` does scan, maps, generation and build for all programs; a stop (`--player-stop`, or a probe run) adds its target as an entry and maps everything that follows from it, asking Ghidra only as a fallback. All programs are regenerated together (the main program's bindings refused a changed overlay map before).
- **Checked:** unit tests for the three new rules and the scan; baseline hash `5a4960667d4a3dff`; the run with the user's card and a skipped film; the gate.

**Gate:** passed (21 CTests, Python suites, probe runs, 32 comparisons and renders).

## 2026-10-18 — The EE interpreter bridge

Step 3 of the [whole-game coverage plan](../plans/ee/WHOLE_GAME_COVERAGE_PLAN.md), on the user's decision of the same day: code the map does not hold runs interpreted instead of stopping the run, and is logged so the next build compiles it.

- **One meaning per instruction:** the handlers are not written by hand. `generate_interpreter.py` lays the 82,667 distinct non-branch instruction words of the mapped code out as one-instruction functions, generates them with the pinned recompiler and the same source preparation as the game's functions, and makes each body a handler taking the program counter. The interpreter itself decodes only branches and jumps, with the generated code's rules (delay slots, 64-bit comparisons, loop iterations counted at backward jumps within a function, calls and jumps elsewhere through dispatch, `ERET`).
- **A general fix:** a quadword store reached a FIFO when the generator knew its address (`Store128`) and failed when it did not (`WRITE128`, main RAM only); `WRITE128` now takes the EE register window to `Store128`, as doubleword stores already did.
- **Checked:** with every generated function interpreted (`BT2_EE_INTERPRET=all`), the scripted fight (5,100 frames, 2.48 billion interpreted instructions) agrees with the compiled run on all 6,534 fields of the state hash and on the VU1 hash; the normal run interprets nothing; unit tests for the core and the handler generator.
- **Tooling:** report fields `ee_interpreted_entries` and `ee_interpreted_instructions`; the discovery loop maps interpreted entries, also from a played session's log; the battle run fails when anything was interpreted; the game program tells the player when a session ran untranslated code.

**Gate:** passed (CTest, Python suites, probe runs, the battle run, the map closure, 32 comparisons and renders).

## 2026-10-18 — What the game asks of the runtime

Step 4 of the [whole-game coverage plan](../plans/ee/WHOLE_GAME_COVERAGE_PLAN.md): `runtime_census.py` reads the system call numbers, hardware register addresses and VU1 microcode from the mapped code and compares them with the runtime's sources. Of 69 system calls the code reaches, 41 are handled without a gate, 4 are numbers the game installs itself (the report now lists `installed_syscalls`), 5 are handled only under gates, 19 are not named (thread exit and deletion, interrupt-context enable/disable, exception handler installation, leaving the game, `GetMemorySize`, three unknown numbers). 85 register addresses are used, 5 named only under gates. The runtime has 163 conditions on the guest pc, branch addresses, startup phases, call counts or the stack offset: the work list for step 5. The VU1 microcode uses only implemented instructions.

**Gate:** passed (CTest, Python suites, probe runs, the battle run, the map closure, 32 comparisons and renders).

Step 6 of the [whole-game coverage plan](../plans/ee/WHOLE_GAME_COVERAGE_PLAN.md) begun. The main loop loads the overlay, calls its entry (the menus) and then a battle routine; the progress structure (pointer at `0x3876A4`) holds flags at `+0x18` and the mode at `+0x1C`, dispatched through a table of 64 modes in screen groups. In the scripted fight: mode 1 (title to main menu), 30 (Dueling), 31 (characters), 34 (map), then the battle with mode 31. ## 2026-10-18 — The idle demo fight's hang: VCLIP flags

The idle demo fight hung in its first frame in a routine that places points at random and moves each back inside a box by ±80 until a VU0 `VCLIP` reports it inside. The generated `VCLIP` swapped the "above" and "below" bits and compared with `w` instead of `|w|`, so a point outside was moved further out until the step no longer changed it (a call trace showed z = 1.07e9). `prepare_runtime.py` corrects it (`fix_clip`, one site, also in the interpreter's handler). Whether the loop runs away depends on the random points, so this was not a regression.

- **Checked:** unit test of the rewrite; the idle run to flip 13,000 plays the demo fight and returns to the title (4,687 functions entered, nothing interpreted; frames in `work/frames/demo-10300.png` and `demo-12000.png`); the scripted fight never reaches the routine.

## 2026-10-18 — A battle without input

Step 6 of the [whole-game coverage plan](../plans/ee/WHOLE_GAME_COVERAGE_PLAN.md): the battle setup is found (by comparing memory between scripted runs that differ in one choice, and reading the code that writes and reads it), and `BT2_START_BATTLE=<character>[:<variant>],<character>[:<variant>],<map>[,<music>]` starts a CPU-against-CPU battle at once, through the game's own setters (the ones its demo fight calls) and its own battle routine. Tool runs only.

- **What a battle needs from the entry's first run:** the second archive mounted (file numbers from 13,234 up) and the common files loaded (the character table among them); the card check, the logos and the films are left out.
- **Runtime rules generalized** (startup-phase special cases the starter ran into): a busy main thread receives fields before the first presented frame too, the runaway guard counts per field from the start, the no-flip limit counts once a frame was presented or main was busy past a field, and main lowering its own priority below a ready worker yields to it.
- **Checked:** `0:2,70,22` loads without input and fights to a K.O. at about flip 4,000, then the game waits in its post-battle menu (3,624 functions entered, nothing interpreted); the scripted fight's state hash agrees on all 6,534 fields before and after the runtime changes; unit test of the switch's text and the function replacement.

## 2026-10-19 — Story scenes start from their map markers

- **Markers:** the story map ends when the player reaches a marker; the map's exit test asks which marker, runs that marker's block of the scene script (block 2000 + marker), whose command names the next scene, and fades out. Script arguments index the script's values (section `GSDT`). 157 of the 207 parts have a marker to their own story scene.
- **`BT2_START_EPISODE=<episode>,<part>,marker:<m>`** reports the player at that marker once (a tool-only replacement of the two marker queries): episode 0 part 0, marker 14, plays its opening cutscene without input up to the first text box.
- **SOUNDS request `0x80`** (reached by that scene) loads a sound bank into an existing slot and keeps the bank count, as the module's handler does (read from its code); implemented in the sampler and the service, with a unit test.
- **Open, for the next pass:** a tool-only advance of text boxes and of the pre-battle confirmation, so story battles run; the 50 parts without a story marker. The pass ended here (the user's instruction to stop when it gets long).

## 2026-10-19 — Runtime generalization, group 3 (first part): the startup's thread calls

## 2026-10-20 — HD textures drawn

- The GPU renderer draws pack 2's replacements (`BT2_HD_TEXTURES`): the GS sink hands transfers on in pieces that end before uploads and CLUT loads so the texture names stay in step with the renderer; a paraLLEl-GS patch asks for a replacement before caching a texture, addresses it at the texture's original size and never reuses it for another texture; replacements load between frames under the budget.
- Found and fixed a paraLLEl-GS bug the pieces exposed: its whole-loop path for PACKED draws counted the loops of the whole transfer, so a transfer ending inside a draw packet was read past its end (the renderer lost its place in the GIF stream). A GPU test sends a draw in pieces with poison behind each and fails without the fix.
- Measured on the Ultimate Battle Z screens and a fight: 57,784 draws replaced, 159 replacements, 115 MB at the peak, no renderer errors; pictures with and without the pack in `work/frames/hd-textures/`.
- Checked: the gate (32/32 comparisons, battle hash unchanged).

## 2026-10-20 — Super-sampling

- `BT2_SUPER_SAMPLING=<1|2|4|8|16>` sets the GPU renderer's samples per pixel; with 4 or more it scans out at 1,280 × 896. With pack 2 on the Ultimate Battle Z screens and a fight: no renderer errors, 27 s at 4× and 31 s at 16× for 2,000 flips; comparison pictures in `work/frames/hd-textures/`.

## 2026-10-20 — Widescreen

- PCSX2's 16:9 patch for SLUS-21441 (six floats and two `lui` constants) plus the story map's projection width, which that patch leaves at 4:3: `runtime/ee/game_patches.cpp` writes the words after the executable is loaded (each original checked), and `prepare_runtime.py` makes the two instructions (listed in `config/game_patches.json`) read their immediate from guest memory, so a patch is a memory write. `BT2_GAME_PATCHES=widescreen`; Display's "Widescreen" in the launcher and the overlay; the window shows the picture 16:9.
- Checked on a versus fight, the Ultimate Battle Z screens and the story map: wider 3D view at unchanged proportions, 2D screens and the HUD stretched (as with the patch in PCSX2), nothing interpreted, the fight's work per picture unchanged. GDI's filtered stretch measured at about 12 ms per picture, so the window copies pixels until the picture is shown on the GPU.

## 2026-10-20 — Pictures shown on the GPU

- The viewer window shows the renderer's picture through a swapchain on the renderer's device (Granite's WSI): no readback per picture (0.1 ms instead of 2.2 to 3.8), a filtered stretch where it is not a whole multiple (widescreen), the menus and the frame rate text uploaded as pictures; the readback only for snapshots and the in-game menu. `BT2_VIEWER_GDI=1` keeps the old path.
- Measured in a versus fight in the window: super-sampling 1x to 16x all at 30 pictures per second without HD (work 15 to 26 ms); the game's thread waits for the renderer more with more samples; pack 2 adds about 10 ms of texture naming per picture, and at 16x with it the fight drops to 22 to 25 per second: the next performance items.
- Widescreen checked against gameplay: the same CPU-vs-CPU fight to flip 5,000 shows the same moves, hits and health at the same flips with and without it.

## 2026-10-20 — HD textures and super-sampling without waits

- The game's thread waited for GPU frame contexts (34% of its time at 16x with pack 2) while the card was half busy: the renderer and the window each use one per picture and there were 4. With 8, 16x with pack 2 holds 30 pictures per second in the window (work per picture 17 to 21 ms instead of 30 to 39).
- Texture naming made cheap: names per draw only for the naming log, GS addresses from page tables, uploads and readouts row by row, repeated uploads and CLUT loads into unchanged memory skipped. Its share of the game thread fell from about 34% to 13% with the same textures replaced. Tests for the tables (against the sink's functions) and the skipping.

## 2026-10-20 — 60 fps: a fight frame's commands

- `BT2_VIF1_DUMP` records a flip's VIF1 transfers. Two successive fight frames have the same VIF1 structure (12,382 codes in the same order) and the same data except about 100 blocks: per skinned part two bone matrices, a few camera-sized blocks and 150 of 879 DIRECT blocks. So in-between pictures can be drawn from the game's own commands with the matrices halfway, leaving the game's 30 steps per second untouched (the fight identical by construction). Open: drawing them without disturbing the real frame's GS memory.

## 2026-10-20 — 60 fps: an in-between picture drawn

- `BT2_VIF1_REPLACE` swaps a flip's VIF1 list (a copy, never guest memory) for one with the camera and bone matrices halfway between two recorded frames. The picture is a clean in-between of the two real frames; game memory is equal page for page with and without it. Next: drawing such pictures live, in their own GS memory.

## 2026-10-20 — 60 fps: the engine steps by a fixed amount

- With the fight's 30 fps wait lifted mid-fight (`BT2_FIGHT_UNCAPPED`), the fight passes the same steps (pictures pixel-equal at the same flips) in half the time: engine-level 60 is double speed. In-between pictures drawn from the game's commands stay the way to 60 that keeps the fight exactly as it is (half a game frame of delay).

## 2026-10-21 — Play test fixes

- The in-game menu froze the game: its background picture was read back between flips, and the next frame context waited for the command buffer paraLLEl-GS still held; `GpuGsBackend::readback` flushes the renderer first. Reproduced and checked with `BT2_PAUSE_TEST` in a player's run.
- Input lag: the pad was sampled at the VBlank before the paced field's wait; it is now sampled after it (`ee_sound_dispatch.cpp`), so input is at most the field's work old. The game sees the same order (both before its VBlank handlers). A menu press shows four fields later, the game's own pipeline.
- Thin lines pulsing when the picture moves (Cell Games floor tiles, widescreen stretch): the GPU presenter stretches in two steps, whole multiples without filtering, then the rest filtered.
- Integer scaling with widescreen and 4x or more super-sampling: the window (796 per multiple) and the picture (1,593 at 896 lines) disagreed by a pixel, so the picture showed at half size; both widths now come from the height.

## 2026-10-21 — 60 fps battles in the window, a setting

- The in-between pictures run on a worker thread (Granite's second thread index); a second renderer's flushes no longer move the shared device's frame (`parallel_gs_frame_boundaries.patch`), so it can flush while the main renderer's frame is open; the in-game menu and the window presenter submit the main renderer's work before taking the device's next frame.
- The second renderer no longer replays every frame: the main renderer keeps its GPU state after each flip (GS memory with super-samples and the CLUT, `parallel_gs_vram_backup.patch`), and each in-between picture starts from the previous flip's. Drawing the in-between picture into the state the replay continued from had made the second renderer drift (the motion blur took it in twice): the artifacts the user saw. `BT2_SIXTY_FPS=check` (blend 1) is pixel-equal to the main picture.
- VIF1 lists are aligned unpack by unpack (Myers' diff on the codes): whole lists paired in fewer than half the fight frames, aligned ones in 1,474 of 1,499.
- The setting "60 fps battles" on the Display page. A fight holds 30 flips and 60 pictures per second, plain (24 ms of work per flip) and with 16x, HD textures and widescreen (30 ms).
- Correction: the frame recorded up to flip F appears at flip F + 1 (checked pixel for pixel), not F + 2.

## 2026-10-21 — HD textures on the fighters

- Evidence: the game draws the fighters' palettes each frame from base palettes it uploads (pages `0x1E0`, `0x1D6`) and relights them every frame (100 to 260 palettes per body texture in a fight; pack 2 holds 2 to 25). Exact PCSX2 names cover 22 to 29 % of a fight's draws even with the palettes read back exactly; about 44 % are a shading texture the pack lacks.
- The renderer patch now draws a replacement over the texture instead of in its place: exact ones (one palette, or every palette) as they are; the fighters' as detail on the game's colours (the texture's colour times the replacement's brightness over its brightness at the texture's size). The pack looks a texture up by name, then any palette's variant; images are kept by file. `build_parallel_gs_shaders.py` rebuilds the patched shader with glslc (checked pixel-equal on the unpatched source); `fetch_parallel_gs.py` runs it.
- Tried and dropped on the way: palettes read back by fingerprint (exact but the fingerprints never repeat), the nearest learned variant (slow, shading in steps), any variant as it is (wrong colours), exact matches switching with detail (flicker).

The GS command sink keeps the packet checks, the register and transfer tracking and the texture names' lockstep; its GS memory, CLUT, vertex queue and sprite/triangle drawing, the `cpu` and `mirror` probe modes, the CPU-drawn view, `render_gs_frame.py` and the stop report's GS memory dump are gone. ## 2026-10-21 — HD textures a PCSX2 drop-in again; 60 fps steadier

- The user saw artifacts in the menus with the detail method and asked for HD textures as seamless as PCSX2, after checking the Tenkaichi 3 references. Tenkaichi3Decomp uses PCSX2 packs as they are: PCSX2's names (in its pack most palette names hash the used index range) and replacements drawn instead of the originals. Done: the detail method and its shader rebuild dropped; names with the used palette range, then the whole palette; palettes the game draws on the GPU copied after each frame without waiting. A fight's draws found: 22.2 % (4.5 % before). The bodies stay HD only part of the time: read back exactly, their palettes change about a hundred times in a fight against the pack's handful, which PCSX2 cannot match either.
- 60 fps, from Tenkaichi3Decomp's in-between pictures: bone and camera axes blended as turning (lengths kept), no in-between picture across a camera cut, none waited for. Plain: 30 flips and about 60 pictures per second. With HD textures the game's thread needs 33 to 40 ms a flip: the two together do not fit yet.
